      r1 fix round, rc 1, each for the reason its closure names or the pin's own claim names (the
      r1 round's added case reddens at `expect(element).toHaveAttribute("aria-disabled",
      "true")`). Each added pin was mutation-checked against the wrong rule it exists to kill, and
      reddens exactly there:
      dropping the scope key from `pendingOlderLoadFor` reddens "does not let one scope's press in
      flight block another scope's Load older at the same cursor bytes" (`Expected the element to
      have attribute: aria-disabled="false" / Received: aria-disabled="true"`), and both "silence
      every superseded notice" and "silence every non-refusal notice" redden "keeps a non-refusal
      failure's notice when the press no longer owns the chain" (`Expected element to have text
      content: The server is temporarily unavailable. / Received: Messages are saved to this
      context.`); the r1 fix round's two new pins kill their own rules — a `pendingOlderLoadFor`
      comparing boundary ids whenever either side names one (leaving the bytes fallback
      unreachable) reddens "does not admit a Load older at the same cursor bytes while either side
      cannot name a boundary id" alone (1 failed / 47 passed), and a `.finally` clearing every
      press record reddens the busy assertion that case and "does not let one scope's press in
      flight block another scope's Load older at the same cursor bytes" now carry (2 failed /
      46 passed) — while in the other direction a superseded refusal delayed past the r1 round's
      settlement fence reddens "leaves the chain alone when the refused press no longer owns it"
      at `expected 'pending' to be 'error'` instead of passing its negative assertions unrun; each
      mutant reddened only the pins named and left the chat suite's other cases green. The r1
      round also confirmed the re-open's named structural-sharing limit with a throwaway probe
      (deleted after the run): a byte-identical re-serve kept the applied data object, the note
      stayed "oldest history loaded", and no button appeared. The path-triggered render gate
      (`SYMMETRY_RENDER_GATE_REQUIRED=1`, the seven focused spec files, desktop and mobile Chromium,
      against three release instances of `750a496` serving its bundle on this host):
      93 expected, 1 skipped (the by-design concurrency skip), 0 unexpected, rc 0 (8.5 m), and
      `scripts/ci/assert-playwright-json.py` rc 0 over CI's own expected set; the chat spec's 12
      cases are all expected in it. The release instances were proved to serve the bundle built at
      `750a496`: the asset body's md5 `7d2f0e69…` matched from all three ports, port 4010 measured
      `/portal/assets/index-wiT0iX5k.js` → HTTP 200 with 885870 B, and the pre-apply
      `index-CS3_ZMmm.js` answered HTTP 404 (also measured on 4010). The implement-stage numbers
      measured before the apply — 84 tests, 496 tests, 5 failed / 40 passed (45), bundle
      `index-CS3_ZMmm.js` md5 `45798f9b…`, 93 expected / 1 skipped / 11.8 m — are superseded by
      the apply-stage numbers above; the pre-apply receipt's earlier flaky invocation (92 expected
      / 1 unexpected, the fixture-gated goal-context case under host load) has no counterpart in
      the apply run. The r1 review's own re-measure at `8e91434` hit one flaky case there: the
      untouched, load-sensitive `src/tool-cache-config.test.ts` exceeded vitest's 5 s default
      timeout under suite parallelism (twice; green in isolation). This fix round's full-suite run
      at the delivered tree passed 20 files / 502 tests, and the isolated file re-measured rc 0
      (2 tests); the flake is recorded as host load acting on an untouched file, not normalized by
      reruns.

## Control instances sharing one worktree's build

- [ ] Two Control instances started from the same worktree share `control/_build`, so a foreign
      session's dev server can answer this worktree's browser runs with `# unhandled` error pages
      (observed 2026-11-17 as console-500 assertions and render-wait timeouts during 0007g's
      suite-context repetitions, with zero offset-assertion failures in the same runs). Give each
      instance its own `MIX_BUILD_PATH` when several run at once; the runs that need isolation
      should do it explicitly rather than relying on nobody else being active.

      **Fixed (2026-11-19, `fix/control-instance-build-isolation`):** both harnesses that start a
      Control instance from a worktree now pin that instance's own build path:
      `browser/scripts/run-planning-tests.ps1` and
      `browser/scripts/run-connection-fixture-tests.ps1` export
      `MIX_BUILD_PATH=<worktree>/control/_build/instances/<harness>-<port>` for every mix command
      they run and set it again on the server (planning) or fixture (connections) command they
      start, so instances from one worktree that both serve compile into different build paths:
      each harness refuses a port that already answers, and a concurrent start on one port fails
      closed at bind/readiness. Deps sources stay shared under `control/deps`; only compiled output
      moves. An inherited `MIX_BUILD_PATH` is overridden and reported, never trusted, because a
      stale one from the surrounding shell is the same accident.
      `browser/scripts/control-instance-isolation.test.mjs`
      (`pnpm --filter symmetry-browser-acceptance test:control-isolation`) drives each real harness
      with a recording `mise` stub and asserts that every mix invocation it makes - the database
      tasks and the instance start alike - received the harness's own instance build path, that two
      ports get two paths, and that a foreign inherited value decides nothing; `browser/README.md`
      records the convention, including the path an operator-started Control server needs when
      another instance may run from the same worktree. The planning harness was run end to end on
      this branch (desktop `-g restores`, `1 passed`) against
      `control/_build/instances/planning-<port>`, with its database tasks, server start, readiness,
      login and staged-asset checks all served from that path (`mix` was reached through this
      host's `mixwrap` toolchain because its `mise` cannot install the pinned Elixir); the
      connection-fixture harness is covered by the stub test only on this host, whose fixed fixture
      database port 55444 has no container here.

## Retro review follow-ups

- [ ] Owner decision: should the served `can_start` exclude goal-managed work items? `portal_json.ex`
      serves `can_start` from five conjuncts with no `goal_managed?` term
      (`control/lib/symmetry_control_web/portal_json.ex:257-260`, beside `goal_managed:` at `:255`),
      while the legacy run endpoint refuses every goal-managed item: `POST
      /portal/api/work-items/:work_item_id/run` (`control/lib/symmetry_control_web/router.ex:86`) →
      `Workspaces.launch_work_item/2` (`control/lib/symmetry_control/workspaces.ex:308`, guard at `:317`)
      → `ensure_legacy_work_item/1` (`:865-869`) → 403 `goal_authority_required`
      (`control/lib/symmetry_control_web/protocol.ex:72-73`). A goal-managed item in the shape plan
      admission creates — backlog, agent assignee, no orchestration task (`goals.ex:6699-6714`) — is
      therefore served `can_start: true` once it is ready in an active project with available external
      work, and the work surface renders "Start run" from
      it (`frontend/src/features/work/control.tsx:306`), which the endpoint then refuses. The same
      payload already fences one flag of the family (`can_retry` delegates to
      `Orchestration.retryable_attempt?/1`, which matches `goal_id: nil` only,
      `control/lib/symmetry_control/orchestration.ex:647-650`), while `can_cancel` is unfenced
      (`portal_json.ex:399`) even though the legacy cancel path applies the same guard
      (`workspaces.ex:345-349`). Decide once whether the served `can_*` flags gain the goal fence or
      whether they deliberately describe the legacy path only and the client gates on `goal_managed`,
      then pin the decision with a serving test.

      **Decided and closed (2026-11-20, owner; branch `feat/can-start-goal-fence`):** option A — the
      server fences the flag family; no backward compatibility (the first release is not shipped).
      Served `can_start` and `can_cancel` are false for every Goal-managed work item, resolved from
      the same `WorkItem.goal_managed?/1` predicate `Workspaces.ensure_legacy_work_item/1` refuses
      with (`control/lib/symmetry_control_web/portal_json.ex`: `work_item/2` reuses the value it
      already serves as `goal_managed`, and `legacy_capabilities/3` applies it to the execution
      projection the command-response `execution/2` shares); the run/cancel refusals, the 403 code,
      `can_retry` and the client (no Goal gating added) are unchanged. Pinned by
      `control/test/symmetry_control_web/controllers/portal_workflow_test.exs`: "a Goal-managed item
      serves no start or cancel control, agreeing with the endpoints' refusal" — the plan-admission
      shape and a queued Goal attempt serve `false` beside the endpoints' 403
      `goal_authority_required`, while the same-shaped legacy item keeps `can_start: true` and its
      queued attempt keeps `can_cancel: true` — beside the existing case's `can_retry: false` pin.
      The published descriptions now state the fence
      (`contracts/v1/workspace-read.schema.json`, `contracts/v1/work-detail-read.schema.json`,
      `contracts/v1/chat-read.schema.json`), with the generated bindings refreshed through the
      repository generator.

- [ ] Decide whether the Goal surface's `resolve_decision` press presents the page's goal version or a
      submit-time served one. `approvePlan` builds its envelope from `query.data`
      (`frontend/src/features/goals/detail.tsx:124`), the page's last settled read, while its sibling
      senders re-read at submit through `servedEnvelope` (`:77`, used by the generic command `:86` and by
      the plan admission `:129`), so a Goal advance between the last settled read and the press makes
      `ensure_preconditions!/2` (`control/lib/symmetry_control/goals/events_receipts.ex:347-363`)
      answer 409 `stale` (`control/lib/symmetry_control_web/protocol.ex:33`) and the card renders
      "The goal changed since this page loaded" (`detail.tsx:187`) for an action the operator would
      simply repeat. The writer
      that advances the Goal row's `lock_version` is identified, which settles the reachability half of
      the original note: every accepted command appends an event through `append_event!/8`
      (`control/lib/symmetry_control/goals/events_receipts.ex:65`, explicit
      `Changeset.optimistic_lock(:lock_version)` at `:134`), the transition
      and reconcile writers go through `Goal.transition_changeset`
      (`control/lib/symmetry_control/goals/schemas.ex:65`), and the amend branch bumps explicitly
      (`control/lib/symmetry_control/goals/commands.ex:458`); no `update_all`/`inc` writer targets the
      `goals` table (the only such sites under `control/lib/symmetry_control/goals/` update `Command`
      rows at `control/lib/symmetry_control/goals/goal_control.ex:395` and `GoalDecision` rows at
      `control/lib/symmetry_control/goals/commands.ex:447`; the
      seven other sites under `control/lib` update `ProjectResource`, `WorkItem`, `Run` and `Command`
      rows), so no writer bypasses the optimistic lock. An asynchronous reconcile advance
      alone can produce the refusal, which is what makes the page-held version worth revisiting; fix
      direction: build the press's envelope from a submit-time read the way the plan admission does,
      re-deriving the presented decision's own version from that same read, or keep the page version and
      record the refusal plus reload as the intended recovery.

      **Decided and closed (2026-11-20, owner; branch `fix/resolve-decision-submit-read`):** option A —
      the press presents the submit-time served read, and the retry-freeze rule stays (the owner's
      Q4=A); no backward compatibility (the first release is not shipped). `detail.tsx`'s
      `decisionEnvelope` builds the `resolve_decision` envelope from a fresh `application.api.goal`
      read taken inside the press: `expected_version`/`expected_revision` come from that read, and
      `expected_decision_version` is re-derived from the same read's served decision found by the
      decision id the card holds; a read that no longer serves that decision sends nothing (the read
      preloads every decision row of the goal, so this narrows the lookup, not a served state). The
      press's two envelopes — resolution, then admission — are frozen per logical intent (decision id,
      option id, proposal hash) until the server answers, so a re-press after a lost acknowledgement
      resends the recorded bytes: `replay_event` replays only a body whose hash equals the recorded
      `request_hash`, and a body rebuilt from a fresh read would carry the post-application pair for a
      command that already applied. The attempt is released on success and on any answer that settles
      it, and kept on a transport failure or an uninterpretable answer — the settle rule the generic
      command already applies. This recovery is usable only until the goal read refetches: the
      committed resolution publishes its goal invalidation before its response is sent
      (`portal_invalidations.ex:28-32`), so a client that refetches first (the resolution's own
      publication, a reconnect, refocus or the fallback read) sees the decision resolved, the approval
      control unmounts (`planApprovalOption`'s `open?` gate) and the frozen attempt keeps no trigger;
      the resolved-but-unadmitted dead end that can leave behind is recorded as an open entry in
      `docs/TODO.md`. Pinned by `frontend/src/features/goals/goal-attention.test.tsx`:
      "builds the decision resolution from a submit-time read so a concurrently advanced goal is not
      sent stale" (red on the pre-fix bytes — the resolution carried the page's settled 4/3 pair and
      the fixture answered the 409 `stale`, so no admission followed; green after: 5/4, then 6),
      "replays the plan approval's decision resolution byte for byte after a lost acknowledgement"
      (pre-fix the press failed earlier, at its first assertion `expect(first.expected_version).toBe(5)`
      — the resolution carried the page's settled pair; green — the re-press repeated the recorded
      bytes under the recorded identity and both commands were acknowledged) and "replays the plan
      approval's admission byte for byte when its acknowledgement is lost" (the only pin of the frozen
      admission half; red when the `attempt.admit ??` reuse is removed — 1 failed | 26 skipped (27) —
      green on the branch). The fix round added two pins of the freeze's release rule: "releases the
      plan approval attempt when the server answers its resolution" — red on `7a55b46`'s `detail.tsx`
      and red when only the answered-error release is deleted (1 failed | 28 passed (29), the case
      alone) — and "does not replay a frozen approval for a different proposal" — red when only the
      freeze-key check is deleted (1 failed | 28 passed (29), the case alone); the latter stays green
      on `7a55b46`'s `detail.tsx` by construction, because the pre-fix press keeps no attempt whose
      bytes could leak into a changed intent. Recorded runs: implement stage (26 cases) 2 failed |
      24 passed (26), exit 1, on `7a55b46`'s `detail.tsx`, and 26 passed (26), exit 0, on the
      implement commit; apply stage (27 cases) 3 failed | 24 passed (27), exit 1, on `7a55b46`'s
      `detail.tsx`, and 27 passed (27), exit 0, on the branch; fix stage (29 cases) 4 failed |
      25 passed (29), exit 1, on `7a55b46`'s `detail.tsx` (the three pins plus the release pin), and
      29 passed (29), exit 0, on the branch.

      **Server-side follow-up (2026-11-21, branch `fix/goal-command-stale-race`):** the Goal row's
      served `version` is no longer `lock_version`. The new `goals.operator_version` (migration
      `20261121000000_add_goal_operator_version.exs`) advances by exactly one per accepted operator
      command and never for the internal reconciliation, settlement, automatic-admission and
      goal-control appends or transition writes this entry identifies, while `lock_version` stays
      the row-level optimistic lock every Goal writer takes. The read model serves
      `version: map_value(goal, :operator_version)`
      (`control/lib/symmetry_control/goals/read_model.ex:192`) and `ensure_preconditions!/2`
      compares it, so the asynchronous advance this entry names no longer answers 409 `stale` for
      an action the operator would simply repeat. Pinned red-then-green by "an internal reconcile
      advance does not refuse an operator command, while another operator's advance still does"
      (`control/test/symmetry_control/goals_test.exs:177`) and by "internal append events never
      advance the operator version the operator read" (same file), which drives the automatic
      admission-blocker, reconciliation-deferral, automatic-admission and task-settlement appends
      and pins that the advance authority is not readable from a caller's options. The changed
      precondition contract is documented in `docs/design/protocol.md` and `docs/design/data.md`.
      #125's option A remains the press's behavior (`decisionEnvelope` reads at submit time); this
      branch's frontend delta is comment and test-name wording only — the `servedEnvelope`,
      `settleGoalRead` and `onSuccess` comments in `detail.tsx`
      (`frontend/src/features/goals/detail.tsx:80-95`) and three renamed cases in
      `goal-attention.test.tsx` — with no behavior, envelope, contract or assertion change.

- [ ] Pin `can_retry: false` for a superseded run's pinned historical entry. The contract states the
      rule (`contracts/v1/chat-read.schema.json:224`: "A superseded run's pinned historical entry always
      answers false", repeated in `work-detail-read.schema.json:142` and
      `workspace-read.schema.json:281`), and the read model serves it
      (`control/lib/symmetry_control/chat/read_model.ex:51-59` forces it for a historical projection),
      but the case that exercises exactly that shape — "historical run chat remains pinned after retry
      and cannot control a newer attempt" (`control/test/symmetry_control/chat_test.exs:644-789`) —
      asserts `historical` (`:734`) and `refute …can_cancel` (`:735`) and never `can_retry`; the file
      contains no `can_retry` occurrence at all, and no other case targets a pinned historical chat
      entry. Add the assertion in a separate maintenance change and keep the case's other assertions:
      the rule is behavioural, so the pin must fail if the historical branch stops forcing the flag.

      **Closed (2026-11-20, branch `fix/control-test-pins`):** `refute
      detail.work_item.execution.can_retry` was added beside the case's `refute …can_cancel` in
      "historical run chat remains pinned after retry and cannot control a newer attempt"
      (`control/test/symmetry_control/chat_test.exs:838`) with every other assertion kept. Mutation
      evidence, measured rather than assumed (all three runs `mix test
      test/symmetry_control/chat_test.exs:746`): removing the whole historical forcing block
      (`chat/read_model.ex:51-65`) reddens the case (`0/1 passed`, exit 2) at `:836`
      `assert …historical` — the block's observable effect at this entry is the historical flag;
      removing only its `can_retry: false` member leaves the case green (`1 passed`, exit 0): the
      pinned projection (`run_projection/2`'s else-branch, `:262-266`) carries no `controls`,
      `runtime` or `retry_runtime_eligible`, `Protocol.task/1` defaults the absent controls to
      false (`protocol.ex:314-320`), `execution_summary/1`'s can_retry gate already answers false
      (`portal_json.ex:404-405`), and `legacy_capabilities/3` (`portal_json.ex:418-426`) only
      conjoins — so all five control members the forcing merges (`:54-58`) are structurally
      unobservable at a pinned entry. Four of the five are absent-key defaults; `can_cancel`'s
      `queued`/`assigned` arm (`portal_json.ex:399-402`) reads the pinned task's state, which is
      `run.state` (`read_model.ex:243-245`), and cannot fire on a superseded run, which is
      always terminal: retry requires a failed or cancelled task (`orchestration.ex:2673`), and
      the cancel and expiry paths persist a terminal run state before the task's
      `attempt_generation` moves past the run's (`orchestration.ex:2787`, `:3229`). Only
      `historical: true` (`:59`) is load-bearing, and that
      finding is recorded here instead of assumed load-bearing. The assertion's failure control
      is the rule violation it guards: a pinned projection that serves
      `retry_runtime_eligible: true` with the forcing member removed fails exactly at `:838`
      (`Expected false or nil, got true`, exit 2) — the pin's discriminating mutation is (c), the
      pinned entry starting to serve retry eligibility.

- [ ] Document how to recover the repository-default test database, or reset it from the test alias.
      The suite's own database is `symmetry_control_test` (`control/config/test.exs:13-15`, where
      `MIX_TEST_PARTITION` is appended as a literal suffix, so a partition run selects a different
      database name that the test alias creates and migrates when absent), and `mix test` runs
      `symmetry.check_test_db`, `ecto.create --quiet` and `ecto.migrate --quiet` first
      (`control/mix.exs:79`). Create and migrate make a missing or
      migration-behind database usable, but nothing drops or truncates one: a pending migration whose
      table already exists fails the alias's migrate step before any test runs. Observed 2026-11-20
      while gating an unrelated Control change: an unpartitioned `mix test` stopped with Postgrex
      `42P07` (`duplicate_table`) on a migration whose table already existed — `schema_migrations`
      named a revision older than the object set — while the same change ran green on a partition
      database. A maintainer reads that as a code
      failure, and the repository documents no recovery: `ecto.reset` exists as an alias
      (`control/mix.exs:70`) but no doc names it for the default test database, and the only documented
      resets belong to harness-owned databases (`docs/runbooks/session-receipt-recovery-e2e.md`). Fix
      direction: document the drop-and-recreate path for the default test database where the suite's
      commands are documented, or add a recreate/reset step to the `test` alias so a stale default
      database cannot decide a local verdict; `MIX_TEST_PARTITION` stays the CI-parallel path it is, not
      the recovery recipe.
      **Closed (2026-11-20/21, branch `fix/test-db-recovery-docs`): documented, not aliased.** Reproduced
      on the throwaway database `symmetry_control_test_tdbrec` on a dedicated disposable PostgreSQL
      18.6 instance, in the documented explicit-name form of the two commands (`env -u MIX_TEST_PARTITION
      POSTGRES_DB=symmetry_control_test_tdbrec` throughout, `MIX_ENV=test` on the drop); fix round 2
      re-ran the sequence with exactly those bytes and these results:
      `mix ecto.create --quiet` and `mix ecto.migrate --quiet` exit 0; deleting the
      `harness_session_attach_receipts` migration's `schema_migrations` row (`20260911000000`) while
      its table exists makes `mix test test/mix/tasks/symmetry_check_test_db_test.exs` stop in the
      alias's migrate step with `** (Postgrex.Error) ERROR 42P07 (duplicate_table) relation
      "harness_session_attach_receipts" already exists` and exit 1 before any test runs;
      `MIX_ENV=test mix ecto.drop` exits 0 (`The database for SymmetryControl.Repo has been dropped`),
      and the same `mix test` then recreates and migrates the database and reports `Result: 11 passed`,
      exit 0. `docs/design/quality.md` (Commands and gates) now documents the recovery beside the
      suite's commands: the database `config/test.exs` resolves (`POSTGRES_DB`, repository default
      `symmetry_control_test`, with `MIX_TEST_PARTITION` appended as a literal suffix), the failure
      signature, the two commands, why `MIX_ENV=test` is load-bearing, and that
      `symmetry.check_test_db` refuses harness-owned databases only (`symmetry_dev` and the
      `symmetry_0007c_/d_/e_` families), and the documented recipe pins its target: it unsets
      `POSTGRES_DB` and `MIX_TEST_PARTITION`, so it always drops `symmetry_control_test`
      (`symmetry_control_dev` and other ambient names are never reached); a different dedicated
      test database is recovered by naming it explicitly in `POSTGRES_DB`. The recipe's default form
      (`env -u POSTGRES_DB -u MIX_TEST_PARTITION`) was exercised end to end by fix round 2 on the same
      instance under a hostile shell (`POSTGRES_DB=symmetry_control_dev`, `MIX_TEST_PARTITION=hostile`):
      its `MIX_ENV=test mix ecto.drop` removed exactly `symmetry_control_test` and never created
      `symmetry_control_dev`; `mix run --no-start` probes print `symmetry_control_test` for that form and
      `symmetry_control_test_x` for the explicit-name form. The `test` alias is unchanged
      (the increment's contract prefers documentation over an alias change), and `MIX_TEST_PARTITION`
      stays the CI-parallel path, not the recovery recipe. Evidence: the recorded before/after runs in
      `answer.md`, and fix round 2's re-run of the same sequence with the documented explicit-name
      bytes — create and migrate exit 0, the stale run exits 1 before any test (`42P07`,
      `duplicate_table`, the pending migration's `CREATE TABLE`), `mix ecto.drop` exits 0 and the
      recovered run recreates and migrates the database (`Result: 11 passed`, exit 0), with the
      throwaway database dropped afterwards and zero residue in `pg_database`. The sequence ran
      against a dedicated disposable PostgreSQL 18.6 instance (`127.0.0.1:5433`), kept because the
      shared 5432 instance was saturated by concurrent agent suites when the sequence was first
      attempted. The recipe's `test` alias body is pinned by
      `control/test/mix/tasks/symmetry_check_test_db_test.exs`, test
      "the storage aliases run the guard before any database work": the pin fails when the
      guard is dropped from the alias head, when `ecto.create --quiet`/`ecto.migrate --quiet`
      is removed, or when the documented order changes (10/11 each) and passes on the delivered
      alias (11 passed). The full Control suite then ran to completion on the `control/` tree
      of `67310cf` with `MIX_TEST_PARTITION=testdbgates`: `Result: 936 passed, 21 skipped`,
      0 failures, seed 815640 (717.5 s); the later merge `8540a7d` changes no `control/` file.

- [ ] Cover the subject batch stream's stdin-write refusal. `subjectGitBatchStream.streamObject`
      (`daemon/internal/workspace/subject.go:744`) marks the stream desynced and wraps the write
      error when `stream.stdin.Write` fails (`:750-751`, via `desync` `:819`; the child's read end is
      gone), and no test pins that branch: the scripted batch child
      (`daemon/internal/workspace/subject_test.go:1114-1147`) serves `frame <variant> <content>`
      (valid, missing, wrong-oid, wrong-type, malformed-size, negative-size, oversized,
      malformed-header, truncated-header, partial-content, bad-trailer, missing-trailer) and
      `blocked-header`, and every case of `TestSubjectBatchStreamRejectsMalformedResponses`
      (`:1315-1379`) writes exactly one request while the child is still alive, so the failure
      surfaces on the response read (`:753`) rather than on the write. The missing case needs a new
      child variant that answers its first request with a complete in-sync frame and then closes its
      read end, with the close ordered so it is observable before the second request (closing before
      the response is written, or an equivalent deterministic wait for the child's exit, works
      because the digest writes its second request only after consuming the first response); a
      variant that only exits after answering can still take the write into the pipe buffer, so the
      refusal can otherwise surface on the response read instead. Every existing
      exit-after-one-response variant answers a refusal that desyncs and aborts the digest on the
      first blob, and the one-response exit at `:1206-1208` covers every variant except `valid` and
      `missing`, which keep serving (`serveWorkspaceBatchFrames` `:1155-1210`); drive a multi-blob
      commit (the fixture exists: `subjectDigestManyBlobs` `:588` with
      `writeSubjectDigestManyBlobsFixture` `:810`) so the second request write lands on the gone
      child. Assert that the refusal names the object, marks the stream desynced and still reaps the
      child (the reap contract `:1373-1376` pins that for response failures), keeping the context
      alive so `streamError` (`:808-813`) reports the pipe error instead of a cancellation.

      **Covered and closed (2026-11-20; branch `fix/subject-stdin-refusal`):** test-only closure —
      the write-refusal branch is correct as implemented, so `subject.go` keeps no diff against
      `fdf332d` and no behavior change is claimed. The scripted batch child gains the
      `close-frame-block` variant (`serveWorkspaceBatchCloseReadEndThenFrame`,
      `daemon/internal/workspace/subject_test.go`): it reads its first request, closes its own stdin
      read end, then writes the complete in-sync frame for that request from the first committed
      entry's real bytes and blocks, so the close is ordered ahead of the response that gates the
      digest's second request and the refusal provably lands on the write rather than the response
      read (`:753`). Pinned by `TestSubjectBatchStreamWriteRefusalDesyncsAndReapsChild` (same file)
      over a `writeSubjectDigestManyBlobsFixture` commit: the digest fails, `stream.desynced` is
      true, the error names the second tree entry (`request object <oid> from Git batch stream:`),
      joins the path's unresolved-process fence, is not a cancellation (context alive), and the
      terminated blocked child is waited for and reaped with `!ProcessState.Success()`. Failure
      control, re-broken on demand: dropping the object name from the refusal reddens the pin on the
      message assertion; dropping `desync` from the branch reddens it on the desynced assertion
      after the test's own `testHangGuard` deadline, because the in-sync `finish()` route cannot end
      a blocked child; the restored branch is green. Run on Windows and WSL Ubuntu-24.04
      (`go test ./internal/workspace/... -count=1`; plus `-race` on Linux). On the final test bytes
      the loaded Windows host aborted that plain command twice at the default 10m test-binary
      timeout (600.509s/600.388s, 0 test failures), and green came with `-timeout 45m`
      (`ok 1119.686s`); CI's `daemon-windows` job runs the package under `-timeout 600s`.

- [ ] Give `stopDescendantMonitor` the post-timer recheck its sibling join already has.
      `daemon/internal/platform/process_unix.go:752-769` waits on the monitor against a timer
      (`:763-768`) and returns `ErrLinuxDescendantContainmentUnproven` ("descendant monitor did not stop
      before deadline") whenever the timer arm wins, without rechecking `monitorDone`. `monitorDone` is a
      close-only unbuffered channel (`:610` created, `:809` the only close), so it stays ready once
      closed: a monitor that exits as the deadline expires leaves both arms ready, Go picks uniformly
      between them, and a monitor whose published reason is already in hand can be reported as a
      deadline failure. PR #105 (`e64f8c0`) fixed this shape in the barrier join `:665-688`
      (function `waitForInitialDescendantScan` `:642-696`, the recheck at `:682-686`, pinned by
      the join probe `process_unix_linux_test.go:2078` and the expired-timer probe
      `:2136`, whose assertion at `:2157-2158` reddens when the recheck is removed) — and recorded
      this site as out of scope as work "to be recorded in `docs/TODO.md`"; the same shape also sits
      in the final-scan RPC's timer arms (`:1042-1043` request, `:1055` result). Fail-closed, so the
      cost is manufactured
      unproven-containment results for every caller
      that joins the monitor's own reason (`:1121`, `:1277`, `:1351`, `:1542`,
      `containment_supervisor_linux.go:1836`, `prepareDescendantMonitorForOwnerLoss` `:771-794`).
      Apply the same recheck and pin both directions (a monitor that exits by the deadline joins; one
      that never exits still fails) in a separate maintenance change.

      **Closed (2026-11-21, branch `fix/stop-descendant-recheck`): the recheck is applied to
      `stopDescendantMonitor` and both directions are pinned.** The timer arm now rechecks
      `monitorDone` with a non-blocking select — the same shape as the barrier join's `:682-686` — so
      a monitor that exits as the deadline expires is joined instead of being reported as a deadline
      failure, while an open monitor keeps the unchanged deadline error after the full wait and
      success is still reported only when the monitor exited (its only production close is `:817`).
      The entry guard (`remaining <= 0`) is unchanged, as in the barrier join, and the recheck can
      never wait, so no caller's deadline is extended. Decision: the final-scan RPC timer arms named
      above are deliberately not changed by this closure — the item's fix sentence pins the stop
      path's two directions only — and are re-recorded as their own open entry in `docs/TODO.md`
      (`## Retro review follow-ups`), with anchors rebound to the fixed tree. Pins, both in
      `daemon/internal/platform/process_unix_linux_test.go`: the expired-timer probe
      `TestProcessGroupStopDescendantMonitorReportsJoinedMonitorAfterDeadline` reddens when the
      recheck is removed (mutant: red 4/4 plain with first failures at iterations 37/29/29/36 of the
      400-iteration 100 ns bucket; red 4/4 under `-race` and 3/3 under `-race -cpu 1` with first
      failures at 12/3/0/1 and 11/6/6 of the 2 µs bucket — the detecting bucket moves with the build
      and the 100 ns bucket alone left `-race` green 3/3, which is why the probe walks a deadline
      ladder instead of one constant), and the deterministic probe
      `TestProcessGroupStopDescendantMonitorJoinsEndingMonitor` pins that an exited monitor is
      joined without waiting while an open monitor still consumes the whole deadline and fails.
      Evidence on the branch, from `daemon/`: WSL Ubuntu-24.04 (go1.27.0) ran the focused probes and
      the ordinary and `-race` suites of `./internal/platform/` green (focused `ok … 0.3 s`; ordinary
      `ok … 21.0 s` with 204 top-level PASS and 0 FAIL; `-race ok … 45.1 s`), with the six TCP-peer
      loopback-dialing tests named-skipped
      (`-skip "^Test(VerifyLoopbackTCPPeerWithOwnedHelperProcess|VerifyLoopbackTCPPeerWaits|VerifyLoopbackTCPPeerDoesNotExtend|VerifyLoopbackTCPPeerV2|LinuxTCPPeerV2RejectsDifferentNetworkCookie)"`).
      That exclusion records the guest's own loopback limitation, not this change: on this host no
      127.0.0.1 connect completes at all (a five-line python listen/connect probe times out), the same
      dials fail or hang identically against `fdf332d`'s own `process_unix.go` in a throwaway copy
      carrying only the new probes, and this file already records that guest behaviour (the
      `daemon/internal/app` intermittent `-race` package-timeout entry names it: the WSL guest hangs
      `TestClaimAdmissionInputFailsClosedBeforeWorkspaceOrProcessStart` on a loopback dial that
      never answers there).
      Windows (go1.27.4): `gofmt -l .` empty, `go vet ./...` and `go build ./cmd/...` green; the
      aggregate `go test -count=1 -timeout 600s ./...` exceeded its package budget in
      `internal/workspace` on this loaded host (600 s panic in
      `TestConcurrentPrepareUsesOneReservation` on a `git` child), and the same suite at
      `-timeout 1200s` failed only `internal/harness/pi`
      (`TestNativeRepositoryTaskSubjectUsesRepositoryBaseline`: "git rev-parse timed out"), both green
      on isolated re-run (`./internal/workspace/` `ok … 456.6 s`; `./internal/harness/pi/` `ok … 5.8 s`
      and the named test `-count=3` `ok … 14.3 s`); neither can come from this change — the changed
      file is `//go:build linux` and absent from the Windows build file set (`go list` lists 14 files
      for `./internal/platform` on Windows, none of them it).

## Retro review follow-ups (second pass, 2026-11-20)

- [ ] Re-point legacy work-surface owner bookmarks that carry the pre-#111 sentinel. The `owner`
      param is read at `frontend/src/features/projects/filters.ts:50` and normalised at `:80-87`,
      and the row comparison sits at `:94` (`ownerKey(item.assignee) === filters.owner`); #111
      (`db31838`) changed the encoder (`frontend/src/features/work/presentation.ts:99-106`): an
      agent row with no recorded name used to mint `agent:unrecorded` and now mints `agent:`
      (`ownerKeyValue` `:94-97` escapes a recorded name that equals the legacy word or starts with
      `:`, so a row genuinely named `unrecorded` mints `agent::unrecorded`), and `human:` keys
      moved the same way; `human:unrecorded` likewise matches no row. A bookmark or hand-typed
      link carrying `?owner=agent:unrecorded` matches no row, while `ownerKeyLabel` (`:110-126`)
      still reads that key as the row's own absence label: the Work owner select
      (`frontend/src/features/projects/work-surface.tsx:141,170`) shows "Agent (not in this
      project)" above an empty list, even when the project has an unnamed-agent row; the label
      side is already pinned, and
      its own case documents the intent the filter does not keep — "labels the legacy sentinel keys
      old bookmarks carry as the absence they named" (`frontend/src/features/work/presentation.test.ts:81-89`,
      whose comment reads "a URL that still carries it reads as the absence it named rather than as
      a recorded name") — while the filter case covers only `unassigned`
      (`frontend/src/features/projects/filters.test.ts:52-59`, the owner case at `:56`), never the
      legacy value.
      Reproduced at `978abba` against the repository's own modules (a throwaway Node ESM probe
      that imports `filters.ts` and `presentation.ts` through a type-stripping resolve hook; no
      test file added and no dependency install): `ownerKey` printed `"agent:"`,
      `"agent::unrecorded"` and `"agent:builder"` for those three assignees (rows built as
      `{key, title, status: "ready", blocked: false, assignee}` with assignees
      `{type: "agent", name: null, agent_profile: null}`,
      `{type: "agent", name: "unrecorded", agent_profile: null}`,
      `{type: "agent", name: "builder", agent_profile: "builder"}`),
      `filterWorkItems(rows, filtersOf({owner: "agent:unrecorded"})).length` was `0` of `3`,
      `ownerKeyLabel("agent:unrecorded")` printed `"Agent"`, and the currently minted key `agent:`
      matched `1` of `3`. Fix direction: read a filter key through one normalising reader so the
      legacy sentinel word maps to the empty-value sentinel the encoder now mints (or redirect the
      legacy value at the surface), and pin the compatibility case in
      `frontend/src/features/projects/filters.test.ts`. Suggested owner area: portal
      work-surface/frontend.

      **Fixed (2026-11-20, branch `fix/legacy-owner-bookmarks`):** the entry's first-named
      direction, one normalising reader. `ownerKeyFilter`
      (`frontend/src/features/work/presentation.ts:127-139`) maps the legacy sentinel word to the
      empty-value sentinel the encoder mints now (`agent:unrecorded` → `agent:`,
      `human:unrecorded` → `human:`) and returns every other key as written, so the escaped
      `agent::unrecorded` #111 added stays a recorded name and no legacy spelling collapses onto
      it; the reader is a fixed point on every key it returns. Both filter-key reads go through
      it: `workSearch`'s `owner` param (`frontend/src/features/projects/filters.ts:50-51`), the
      reader the router's `validateSearch`, every toolbar `setSearch`, `readSavedViews` and the
      work detail's `listState` share — so a bookmark that still carries the pre-#111 spelling
      reaches the surface, the toolbar's absent-owner check and `sameWorkSearch` as the minted
      key — and `filtersOf`'s filter-state build (`:85-87`), so a search that never passed the
      URL parser re-points as well. The visible effect on the entry's second symptom: the select
      now matches the minted key against `ownerOptions`, renders the row's own "Agent" option,
      and labels the absent fallback from the minted key instead of duplicating the legacy one.
      No surface, label, encoder, storage-schema or contract change; the decision and the

      Pinned by `frontend/src/features/projects/filters.test.ts`, case "re-points the legacy

- [ ] Align the control-character escape case in the canonical JSON bytes both languages hash.
      `protocol.CanonicalizeJSON` (`daemon/internal/protocol/goal_subject.go:102`) re-encodes every
      string through `encoding/json` (`writeCanonicalJSON` `:122`, its string arm `:132-137`;
      `marshalJSONString` `:186` with HTML escaping off), and Go spells every control character
      below U+0020 that has no shorthand escape as `\u00xx` with lowercase hex digits, while the
      Elixir side hashes `SymmetryControl.RequestHash.canonical/1`
      (`control/lib/symmetry_control/request_hash.ex:5`, `Jason.encode!/1`; Jason 1.4.5 per
      `control/mix.lock:10`) and Jason spells the same character `\u001F` with uppercase hex.
      Each language is self-consistent — in Go both
      `{"value":"\u001F"}` and `{"value":"\u001f"}` canonicalize to `{"value":"\u001f"}` — so the
      defect is the pair: one logical value carries two canonical digests, one per language.
      Reproduced at `978abba` with throwaway probes (not committed): a Go test over
      `CanonicalizeJSON` printed `{"value":"\u001f"}` with
      `sha256:b43e9944b5d28b7f3e6bad4b289642d1786aa5ba80658cf2ed8bf87ecd642e51`, and
      `Jason.encode!(%{"value" => <<0x1F>>})` printed `{"value":"\u001F"}` with
      `sha256:520bd0e56ee01fe3ec0b1c8d5ac7cfc0c2aea0999e9de295553abe6e45dcff63`. The Elixir reading is
      not a lone choice: the contracts layer already mirrors it deliberately — `encodeJsonString`
      (`contracts/ts/semantics.ts:69-80`) emits the shorthand escapes plus an uppercase `\u00XX`
      for every other C0 control, documented as "exactly as Elixir `Jason` encodes it", and the pin
      is the boundary test "canonical JSON escapes control characters exactly as the control
      plane's Jason does" (`contracts/scripts/boundary.test.mjs:59-71`, U+000B and U+001B inside a
      schema-valid LongText description), which names `RequestHash.canonical/1` as the hashed text;
      `contracts:check` verifies the canonical sorted JSON Subject hash through that same TypeScript
      encoder (`contracts/README.md:62-66`). So the Go canonicalizer is the side that disagrees
      with the other two, and it is unpinned: its escaping test pins `\u0000` and the shorthand
      escapes but no `\u00xx` escape carrying a hex letter
      (`daemon/internal/protocol/goal_subject_test.go:64-73`),
      and a case-sensitive search for an uppercase-hex control escape
      (`grep -rn 'u00[0-9]*[A-F]' daemon/ --include='*.go'`) returns 0 lines. The normalization the
      hash must follow is written at `docs/design/protocol.md:311-316` and does not name the C0
      escape case, so deciding it stays the open choice.
      Reachable through the context snapshot: Control builds it from free text — `approved_goal`
      `"objective"` (`control/lib/symmetry_control/goals.ex:2151`) and `work_contract`
      `"title"`/`"description"` (`:2156-2157`) — and stamps the snapshot's `content_hash` with
      Jason (`context_content_hash` `:2602-2606`, via `RequestHash.canonical`), which the daemon
      recomputes with Go and refuses on mismatch
      (`daemon/internal/control/validation.go:1744-1772`, "context.content_hash does not match
      canonical snapshot"; the same rule at `daemon/internal/protocol/goal_schema.go:48-70`).
      The contracts boundary test states U+000B/U+001B are schema-valid inside a LongText
      description (`contracts/scripts/boundary.test.mjs:59-71`), so a work item whose description
      carries U+001F yields a snapshot the daemon rejects — a source-traced consequence, not
      executed end to end in this record. The `Subject` the daemon binds cannot carry C0
      characters (its fields are a UUID, a git object id and a sha256, `Subject.Validate`
      `goal_subject.go:39-47`; `Subject.Hash` `:81-88`), and `goals.ex:2636` hashes that same
      restricted value, so neither is the reachable seam. Fix direction: decide the canonical
      case once — the spelling the other two implementations already share (uppercase, i.e. Jason's
      bytes) or a shortest-escape normalization written into the rule itself — then add the missing
      Go pin (and a shared fixture if the choice tightens the rule), and treat any recorded digest
      over a control-bearing value as invalidated by the change. Suggested owner area: daemon
      protocol plus the Control hash owners, with the contracts owner for the normalization text.

      **Decided and closed (2026-11-20, branch `fix/go-canonical-c0-case`):** option (a) — Go
      adopts the spelling the other two implementations already share (uppercase `\u00XX`, i.e.
      Jason's bytes); `RequestHash.canonical/1`, the contracts TypeScript encoder and their pins
      stay the reference, and the normalization rule now names the case
      (`docs/design/protocol.md:311-320`). `normalizeJSONStringEscapes`
      (`daemon/internal/protocol/goal_subject.go`, formerly `normalizeJSONStringLineSeparators`)
      uppercases the hex digits of every surviving C0 escape token (00-prefixed) in the same
      scanner that keeps `U+2028`/`U+2029` raw and leaves a literal `\\u001f` text unchanged; the
      shorthand escapes, key order and numeric handling are unchanged, so only the nine C0 controls
      whose escape carries a hex letter (`U+000B`, `U+000E`, `U+000F`, `U+001A`-`U+001F`) change
      bytes. Pinned by `TestCanonicalizeJSONEscapesControlCharactersLikeTheControlPlane` and
      `TestCanonicalizeJSONPlanProposalHashMatchesTheControlPlaneDigest`, red before
      the fix (lowercase hex where uppercase was wanted, both tests, exit 1) and green after: the
      first pins the entry's value (`{"value":"\u001F"}`, the digest Jason 1.4.5 computes,
      `sha256:520bd0e56ee01fe3ec0b1c8d5ac7cfc0c2aea0999e9de295553abe6e45dcff63`), the nine
      hex-letter escapes, the document the contracts boundary test pins
      (`sha256:401bf74e8085ac0cce504b465cabbc82043e4fc8c6ec73625d60a43292656fd8`) and the literal
      `\\u001f` text; the second pins the canonical plan-proposal text the daemon recomputes
      before admission (`goal_contract_semantics.go` `canonicalPlanProposalSHA256`) against the
      control plane's digest the frontend pin already records for a description carrying
      U+000B/U+001B (`sha256:6a41b487e2e260e048b11ea7e3221f53aa9705fc5a9d943a353e6b8c39d202e5`).
      A third pin, `TestNormalizeJSONStringEscapesLeavesNonC0TokensUnchanged`, holds
      the 00-token scope of the rewrite itself (it reddens against a rewrite of every four-digit
      token).
      The reproduction no longer reproduces: the post-fix Go canonicalizer's bytes
      equal Jason 1.4.5's byte for byte over the entry's value, the nine hex-letter controls, that
      boundary document and the plan proposal above; the digit-only C0 escapes are unchanged
      because the rewrite only maps the hex letters a-f, and U+0000 stays pinned by the existing
      escaping test. No repository-asserted digest or fixture over affected bytes is invalidated:
      no hex-letter C0 escape predates this change in any daemon source, test, fixture or testdata
      file, and the uppercase pins named above stay valid. Two recorded digest classes are
      superseded rather than reused: the pre-fix probe digest quoted above (its value now
      canonicalizes to the uppercase bytes) and persisted daemon-side digests over affected bytes
      such as `ProviderActionIntent.RequestDigest` (`daemon/internal/app/pi_provider_bridge.go`,
      replayed at `daemon/internal/state/provider_action.go`), where a pre-fix journal fails
      closed with `local_idempotency_conflict` and no duplicate external effect; backward
      compatibility is waived because the first release is not shipped. The reachable end-to-end
      refusal stays source-traced; the plan is `docs/plans/2026-11-20-go-canonical-c0-case-plan.md`.

      `_UNC_PATH` `:142-145` (`_PLUS`), `_SLASH_UNC_PATH` `:167-169` (`_PLUS`),
      `_FILE_URL_PATH` `:204-212`
      (the root-relative `_ROOT_RELATIVE_PATH` `:191-194` and the file-URL host-root arm `:209` do
      not), so a recorded `\n` escape that follows a Windows path is consumed into the rendered
      token together with the text behind it. The POSIX family already fixed exactly this
      shape (`_POSIX_BYTE` `:122`, whose comment at `:116-121` names the failure — "consuming it would
      swallow a JSON escape sequence together with the text behind it" — and
      `scripts/ci/test-model-routing-record-sanitize.py:282-295`,
      `test_a_json_escape_after_a_path_survives_the_render`, STD-A07); the Windows family has no
      such case. Reproduced at `978abba` with the tool on two scratch inputs of one shape (a JSON
      string recording a path, then the two-character `\n` escape, then the word `FAILED`; the
      Windows input's path is drive-rooted and carries the JSON-escaped doubled separators). The
      inputs, one JSONL line each, are built in Python with `BS = chr(0x5C)`: Windows
      `'{"output":"wrote C:' + BS*2 + 'Users' + BS*2 + 'op' + BS*2 + 'run' + BS + 'nFAILED"}'`,
      POSIX `'{"output":"wrote /' + 'tmp/run' + BS + 'nFAILED"}'`. The Windows input renders to a
      single token whose digest is
      `path-sha256:a3a19f2b70d9c277fa8b2ef0f62c217841c3f41ed7e6a78b689045338e4ad1e5`, and that
      digest is the recomputed sha256 of the raw span the token bound — the path text *plus* the
      escape *plus* `FAILED` — where a path-only render of the same input would carry
      `26e7b5b4…` (the sha256 of the path text alone); the POSIX input of the same shape keeps the
      escape and the word outside its token (`…path-sha256:91525b09…` followed by the escape and
      `FAILED`). Consequence in a record: an evidence line behind the escape disappears from the
      rendered bytes while the record stays valid JSON — the loss STD-A07 fixed on the POSIX side.
      Fix direction: the POSIX rule is not directly portable (a raw Windows spelling uses one
      backslash per separator, so a separator run can be shaped exactly like a JSON escape —
      `\run` starts with `\r`), so the reading must be decided — either a JSON-aware pass that
      respects string escapes (the tool's input is recorded JSON text), or a tail that stops
      before an escape-shaped backslash run once at least one separator has been consumed — and
      both the escaped-escape case and the raw single-backslash spelling must be pinned in the
      tester. Suggested owner area: `scripts/ci` record tooling.

      **Closed (2026-11-21, branch `fix/sanitizer-json-escape`), with the PR #119 round-5
      follow-up closed in the same change and the fix round of the same day replacing the first
      design:** the entry's first-named reading, a document-level JSON reading shared by every
      path family (POSIX, drive, UNC, slash-UNC, `file:` URL, root-relative). The second reading
      it names — a tail that stops before an escape-shaped backslash run once a separator has
      been consumed — cannot be correct on its own, and the pins below hold that boundary against
      it: a raw Windows spelling separates its segments with exactly that shape (`\run` starts
      with `\r`, `\tmp` with `\t`), so it would cut a raw location at its last separator and
      leave the segment name standing. The delivered design: `leading_boundary(excluded)` is the
      one leading lookbehind every family that takes one carries — the escape alternative the
      round-4 `--root` rule already had
      (`(?<=\\[nrtbf])|(?<=\\[u][0-9A-Fa-f]{4})`, next to the family's own negative set) — so a
      location behind a recorded escape is a match in every family, `_root_patterns` stops
      spelling its own copy of the same atom, and the round-5 reproduction
      (`printf '%s' '{"out":"ok\n/home/runner/work/x failed"}' | … --check --root-only`, exit 0
      before) now exits 1. (`_COLON_POSIX_PATH` is the one family without the shared atom: its
      `(?<=:)` decides between an absolute path and a URL authority from the very next
      character, which no negative set can do.) The text's own JSON structure then decides the
      reading, once per document: a
      record whose whole text parses with `json.loads`, or whose every non-empty line does
      (JSONL), is read through its string literals — `_decode_literal` decodes each literal with
      the JSON grammar's own escapes and maps every decoded character to the raw span that
      spelled it (a surrogate pair covers twelve recorded bytes) — and `_render_passes` runs the
      matchers on the decoded text, maps each match back to its raw span, escapes inside it
      included, and renders that span, so a digest still binds the recorded spelling and the
      rendered record stays valid JSON. The two readings carry their own byte classes from one
      compiler (`_compile_patterns(classes, guard)`): the raw reading keeps the recorded text's
      whitespace and quote/punctuation set, while the decoded reading refuses the control
      characters and the plain space, the quote, backtick, comma, semicolon, colon and `)` — so a
      space, NBSP or U+3000 a writer escaped continues a location and an escaped apostrophe is a
      path byte, while a decoded newline or tab (a control escape) is a boundary, and a tail
      never absorbs a following escape or the line behind it. A matcher never binds an earlier
      token's text: a match that overlaps a replacement maps back to that replacement's raw span
      and renders that span's own token (the pass coalesces the spans, so nothing is emitted as a
      composite), and the `--root` matchers carry one more boundary, a rendered
      `<disposable-root>` token's own text, so rendering rendered text is a no-op. Everything
      else is prose, which keeps the base tool's raw reading, the shared escape arm, the
      trailing-backslash-before-quote rule and the disclosed over-approximations (space-continued
      chunks and raw spellings whose separators read like escapes). Accepted boundaries, pinned
      rather than silent: in a JSON literal an escape whose spelling also reads as a Windows
      separator is an escape (`\repo` is a carriage return and `epo`), so a mixed spelling —
      valid JSON, because `\r` is a grammar escape, but one no correct producer writes — renders
      the path up to the escape and leaves the escape's own spelling behind. The r2 rule closes
      that first version's fail-open side, extended by round 3's union decision: a raw
      single-backslash spelling inside a valid literal decodes its separators into control
      characters (`{"note":"see C:\repo\tmp"}`), so the decoded value carries no location — and
      the Windows-shaped families (drive, UNC and slash-UNC, root-relative, `file:` URL) then
      run over the literal's recorded bytes, where **every match the scan reads contributes the parts of its
      span that no decoded claim and no rendered token's own text covers**, and those parts are
      unioned with the claims (overlapping or touching spans become one token) — **a raw match
      is never discarded because a decoded span overlaps it**, which is what the review's P1
      table found the r2 filter doing: `{"p":"C:/Users` + the newline escape + `repo"}` left
      its segment names literal after one render and `--check --root-only` refused the tool's
      own render (exit 1). A match with no escaped backslash pair is a raw spelling through all
      its parts, so that shape renders whole (`C:/Users` + the newline escape + `op` + the
      carriage-return escape + `repo`), and the escape and text behind a forward-slash spelling
      are absorbed with it (`//server/share/run` + the newline escape + `FAILED`) —
      over-redaction, the fail-closed direction, the consequence round 3 accepted. A match
      carrying an escaped backslash pair is a producer mix, and the decoded reading's render
      governs its one-escape tails: its uncovered parts with a backslash beyond their opening
      escape character render whole (`C:` + the escaped pair + `Users` + the newline escape +
      `op` + the carriage-return escape + `repo` renders whole), while the entry's own properly
      escaped reproduction keeps the escape and `FAILED` outside the token and the mixed
      spelling `C:\\a\repo` keeps `\repo` outside. A raw match whose span carries no backslash
      is left to the decoded reading (the raw look exists for the single-backslash spellings; a
      recorded DEL byte, which only the raw tail admits, is the discriminating shape). The scan
      no longer uses `finditer` — a location whose tail swallowed the next location's first
      bytes was invisible to one render and found by the next, the non-idempotence the review's
      fuzz counted — and re-enters only `_CONTAINED_RESUME` bytes behind each match it keeps
      (round 4 made the loop linear: a start deeper inside the kept match consumes the same tail
      run and adds no byte, while a crossing start — the second location's prefix straddling the
      stop that ended the first match, round 4's P2 pin — sits at most ten bytes back: the widest
      `_DEVICE_PREFIX` spelling, four backslashes, `?`, four backslashes, plus the drive letter
      behind it — round 4's literal eight was too small for that namespace and the r5 round
      derives the window from the pattern text, where the round-4 review's 10-byte straddle is
      the pin; a `file:` URL's scheme colon sits four bytes back), and
      `_align_span` snaps every replacement's start to the backslash run it splits, so a token
      behind an odd run cannot spell `\p` and the rendered literal still parses (the review's
      fuzz found 14 invalid renders in the pre-round-3 bytes and none at the delivered bytes).
      The families read a second time, anchored at each claim's end, over the decoded reading's own
      rebuild (`_render_passes`' output text — the text the next render reads), because a `--root`
      token's end exposes a spelling the byte it replaced hid: the round-5 review's P2 shape (a root
      immediately followed by `//child` and an escape) renders as one token in the first pass, where
      the pre-fix bytes left the spelling behind the token and the next pass bound the token's own
      text into a new digest — the documented `--root` publish flow wrote a record the
      `--check --root-only` gate refused. The anchored attempts follow the scan's own containment
      rule (a start deeper inside a kept match consumes the same tail run the kept match's end
      already reaches) and contribute the recorded bytes behind the claim under the pair rules.
      `_RAW_SPELLING_ESCAPE`
      gates the scan per literal — an odd backslash run whose last backslash begins one of the
      escapes `n`, `r`, `t`, `f`, `b`, `u` (round 3 fixed the predicate, which saw only runs of
      one) — and also keeps a long non-matching backslash run from being scanned twice; POSIX
      spellings are not re-read — their separators carry no backslash, so the decoded reading
      already reads them. A `--root` token keeps its trailing structure, and a raw spelling of
      a `--root` renders as the Windows family's hashed token rather than `<disposable-root>`
      (the root's bytes are removed either way). All of them are pinned in the JSON module so a
      later narrowing is a deliberate decision.
      Pins live in the split suites — `scripts/ci/test-model-routing-record-sanitize.py` (57
      cases, the raw reading's pattern and CLI battery) and its sibling
      `scripts/ci/test-model-routing-record-sanitize-json.py` (20 cases, the JSON reading - the
      round-4 methods and the round-6 CLI `--root` publish-fixpoint pin included; the
      split keeps both tester files under the 1000-line decomposition threshold, the JSON module loads
      the raw module by path for the shared fixtures, and both run in the same `ci.yml` step and
      README gate list). JSON module: "a JSON escape after a Windows path survives the render" —
      the entry's own Windows input renders `path-sha256:26e7b5b4…` (the digest the entry records
      for the path text alone) with the `\n` and `FAILED` outside the token, in both scopes; "a
      JSON escape after a POSIX path survives the render" (both spellings of the newline, the
      token binding the recorded path alone); "a JSON escape after every Windows family survives
      the render" (the UNC spelling with its escaped leader, the root-relative spelling, the
      `file:` URL's backslash spelling and the escaped-backslash shape `...\\n`, which decodes to
      two literal backslashes and stays one path, all keeping the escape outside the token, plus
      the forward-slash slash-UNC authority and `file:` URL spellings whose whole spans the
      union absorbs, round 3's accepted over-redaction); "an escaped
      space, NBSP and apostrophe stay inside the location" (the four truncated-render shapes and
      a space-continued NBSP location, exact one-token renders); "an escaped quote and surrogate
      pair keep the mapping" (the escaped-quote shapes, the odd-escaped-quote-count literal and
      a surrogate-pair location); "the root matchers escape their own recorded bytes" (a
      `)`-bearing root matches its own spelling and never the record's `\n` escape, a
      rendered token is not re-claimed, a forward-slash drive root keeps its
      `<disposable-root>` trailing structure, and a raw drive root renders as the drive
      family's hashed token); "a token never binds another token's text" (the
      `\u0008`/`file:` composite shape renders two recorded spans with no orphaned `:<hex>`
      fragment, the prose shape merges into one token, and the `//r` + escape + root spelling
      hashes the record's own bytes; round 4's P3 pin: a raw spelling glued in front of an
      existing `path-sha256:<64 hex>` token renders the spelling's bytes as its own token and
      leaves the existing token byte for byte, so the token-spans-not-in-`covered` mutation
      fails); "the reading is decided at the document level" (JSONL
      decodes every line, one prose line makes the document prose, a fenced Markdown block stays
      prose); "a raw single-backslash spelling inside JSON is rendered" (the r2 rule replacing
      the D2b carve-out: the five raw drive shapes whose segments begin `r`, `t`, `n`, `f` and
      `b` render as one whole-span token in both scopes, and the escape-followed shape renders
      the escape and the line with them); "a host path after a JSON escape is caught" (the
      round-5 reproduction over the seven host roots and six escape spellings, the four Windows
      families and the general POSIX family behind an escape, and the boundary negatives); "a
      host path in a JSON key is rendered" (round 3's P3 pin: the N11 mutation, which left a
      literal whose next non-space character is `:` untouched, stayed green before it — a key
      renders like a value in a whole document and in each JSONL line); "a raw match without a
      backslash is left to the decoded reading" (round 3's P3 pin for the raw look's
      no-backslash filter, which the review's N13 mutation removed without a failure: a
      Windows-shaped match carrying no backslash is bytes the decoded reading reads as written,
      and the recorded DEL byte, which only the raw tail admits, shows the decoded reading's
      boundary decision governing); "a wide record is rebuilt once per pass not once per match"
      (round 3's P2 pin: the number of `_rebuild_with_groups` calls for 1,000 and 8,000 matches
      is the same in a literal and in prose, so the count cannot grow with the matches); "a raw
      location inside an earlier match is still rendered" (round 4's P2 pin: the two review
      shapes whose second raw location begins inside the first match's span - `C:\f` + `C:\t` +
      `srv` + the escaped pair + `xfile` + the trailing colon, and the space-continued
      `\r`-then-`\n` run - each assert the exact one-token render, the digest of the union
      span's recorded bytes and the fixpoint, so the restart-at-match-end mutation fails them);
      "a wide raw listing is scanned once per location" (round 4's P1 pin: on the
      `json.dumps` listing of N UNC paths the raw look's searches cover ~N characters, not
      ~N squared - 8x the lines stays under 16x the covered characters and under 4x the
      literal's own length, with at most 64 searches for the whole listing, and the
      rendered one-token-per-line bytes and their fixpoint are checked too); "the
      fail-closed property holds for every repro shape" (thirty-three shapes — the four truncated
      renders, the space fail-open shapes, the token-prefix shape, the stray-quote document, the
      root-metacharacter root, the entry's input, the round-5 POSIX path, a surrogate pair, a
      doubled backslash, and the twelve r2 raw-Windows shapes: the five raw drive spellings, the
      escape-followed one, both UNC dialects with a raw separator, the raw root-relative and
      `file:` URL spellings, the forward-slash spelling, and a mixed literal carrying one
      escaped and one raw location — plus round 3's six: the forward-slash prefix continued by
      raw separators (`{"p":"C:/Users` + the newline escape + `op` + the carriage-return escape
      + `repo"}`, the review's own J1 shape), its JSONL variant, the escaped-pair prefix
      continued by raw segments, the odd-run raw suffix (the guard fix's shape), the
      key-carrying path, and the backslash-run split (the alignment shape, whose render is one
      token and parses) — each: the input
      refuses `--check --root-only`, the render is a
      fixpoint, the rendered bytes pass the same check, and no decoded string carries a fragment
      of the input's host path); and the CLI cases "root-only check refuses a root after a JSON
      escape" (three spellings, including the four-hex-digit newline), "root-only check refuses a
      host path after a JSON escape" (the round-5 command shape exits 1, the default scope
      refuses the same bytes, every shape's render passes the check it refused and survives a
      re-render, and `aa/home/runner/x` still passes) and "CLI renders JSON records to bytes its
      own check accepts" (the publishing round trip), and round 6's "CLI root render then check
      is a fixpoint" (a `--root` immediately followed by `//child` and an escape renders as one
      token in both scopes, `--check --root-only` accepts the output with and without the
      `--root`, and a second render is byte-identical — the round-5 P2 publish flow). The raw
      module keeps the pattern, quoted, URL, space-continuation and CLI battery, and its own
      pins: "a host path after a raw escape spelling is caught" (`ok\t/tmp/run` and `ok` + the
      `u0020` escape + `/tmp/symmetry-run`, both scopes: the two `leading_boundary` escape arms
      no JSON-reading pin needs, because a decoded control character is a boundary by itself),
      and the round-6 M9b pin `test_a_group_ending_at_a_claims_end_binds_its_whole_raw_span` (a
      merged group ending exactly at a rendered root token's end binds its whole raw span; the
      `bisect_left(ends, index)` respelling of `raw_end_of` over-binds the colon the render left
      outside).
      Mutation checks (scratch copies of both suites beside a mutated tool, deleted afterwards;
      every `FAIL:`/`ERROR:` case counted, subtest-keyed and method-level alike, and every number
      below measured in this round):
      - Pre-fix bytes (`git show 7a71383:scripts/ci/model-routing-record-sanitize.py`): the JSON
        module fails 12 of the 17 cases the suite had at round 3 — the Windows-escape,
        every-family, host-path-after-
        escape, root-metacharacter, token-opacity, quote/surrogate, document-level, no-backslash
        raw-match, property, CLI host-path-after-escape and CLI round-trip pins, and the
        wide-record pin errors (no `_rebuild_with_groups` exists in those bytes) — and the raw
        module fails its escape-arm pin, so every round-2 and round-3 fix pin is falsified by
        the bytes it replaces or by the round-3 mutations below. The five cases the pre-fix
        bytes already satisfy: the POSIX-escape case (the POSIX side never had the defect), the
        escaped space/NBSP/apostrophe case (the raw reading over-absorbed there but did not
        truncate), the raw single-backslash spelling pin (the pre-fix raw reading rendered
        those shapes whole, which is why the r2 look was added to the decoded path), the key pin
        (the pre-fix raw reading renders a key too), and the round-4 CLI root-after-escape case
        (its fix predates the merge base).
      - Head bytes (`bda373e`): the JSON module fails six cases (the truncated-render,
        token-opacity, root-metacharacter, decoded-control-spelling, property and CLI round-trip
        pins) — the leak shapes this round closes — and the raw module passes.
      - Round-3 named mutations, measured against the delivered suites (each number is its
        failing methods; the raw module passes on every one, because every mutation edits the
        literal-only path): restoring the r2 drop-whole-match filter fails five JSON cases (the
        entry's Windows digest, every-family, quote/surrogate, document-level and CLI
        round-trip pins); dropping the no-backslash filter fails the no-backslash pin exactly;
        the keys-skipped mutation (the review's N11) fails the key pin and the property corpus;
        reverting `_RAW_SPELLING_ESCAPE` to the run-length-1 predicate fails the property corpus
        (the odd-run shape); rendering pair-bearing parts without the further-separator filter
        fails five cases (the entry's Windows digest, every-family, quote/surrogate,
        document-level and CLI round-trip pins — the properly escaped shapes it absorbs);
        taking the union over whole matches fails the same five; and dropping the span
        alignment fails the property corpus (the split-run-boundary shape, whose rendered
        literal stops parsing as JSON without it).
      - Round-4 named mutations, measured for this round against the delivered suites (the raw
        module passes on each, because each edits the literal-only path): resuming the raw
        look's scan at each match's end (the review's M6, `start = match.end()`) fails exactly
        the new P2 pin's two cases (`C:\f` + `C:\t` + `srv` + the escaped pair + `xfile` + the
        trailing colon, and the space-continued `\r`-then-`\n` run); removing the rendered
        tokens' own text from the raw look's `covered` set (the review's M11) fails exactly the
        glued-token case's two subtests (`C:\temp\run` + `path-sha256:` + 64 `a`s, both
        scopes); and the round-3 bytes' scan (the pre-round-4 `start = match.start() + 1` loop,
        `sha256:75e01391…`, the round's own delivered sanitizer) fails the new scaling pin
        outright — the same counting pin over the same `json.dumps` listing measured 12,004,895
        matched characters at 1,000 lines and 799,578,395 at 8,000 (a quadratic ~64x ratio,
        against the pin's 16x bound and its 4x-of-length absolute bounds), and the single case
        took 433 s on those bytes against the delivered bytes' ~1 s.
      - Named mutations: dropping the document gate (the reading is always prose) fails seven
        JSON cases; compiling the decoded reading with the raw byte classes fails three (the
        escaped space/NBSP/apostrophe case, the CLI round trip, the property); breaking the
        escaped-quote parity (`_string_literal_end` advancing one character after a backslash)
        fails the quote/surrogate case and two raw-module quoted cases; dropping the root-token
        guard fails the root-matcher case; dropping the `leading_boundary` escape arms fails the
        raw module's escape-arm pin and no JSON case (a decoded control character is a boundary
        by itself); mis-mapping a four-hex-digit escape (advancing two characters) fails the
        POSIX-escape, host-path-after-escape and CLI root-after-escape cases; removing the
        apostrophe from the decoded byte classes fails three (the apostrophe case, the CLI round
        trip, the property); removing the space-like characters fails two (the space/NBSP case
        and the property); accepting a decoded newline as a path byte fails seven (the POSIX and
        Windows escape cases, the every-family case, the quote/surrogate case, the root-matcher
        case, the document-level case and the CLI round trip). Disabling the overlap
        merge/absorb rule fails none and is recorded as an equivalent mutant, not as a pass: a
        match over a replacement already maps back to that replacement's raw span and re-renders
        the same token, so the coalescing is what the property pins exercise, and the mutation
        leaves the bytes identical. Every pin therefore has a measured falsifier: eleven JSON
        cases and the raw module's new pin fail on the pre-fix bytes, and the three pre-fix-green
        cases fail under the named mutations (the POSIX-escape case under the accepted decoded
        newline, the escaped space/NBSP/apostrophe case under the byte-class mutations, and the
        round-4 CLI root-after-escape case under the mis-mapped four-hex-digit escape). Round
        4's three new pins each have their own: the P2 pin fails under the restart-at-match-end
        mutation, the token pin under the token-filter mutation, and the scaling pin under the
        round-3 bytes (all measured above). Round 5's two new pins have theirs too, measured at
        the delivered bytes: the device-prefix straddle pin fails on `_CONTAINED_RESUME` windows
        1, 3, 8 and 9 (the derived ten passes), and the root-token-end pin fails under the
        `bisect_left(ends, index)` respelling of `raw_start_of`. Round 6's pins have theirs,
        measured at its bytes: disabling the anchored read (the anchored loop deleted) leaves the
        raw module green and fails exactly the JSON module's new CLI method, the round-5 head's
        own bytes fail the same method, and the
        `bisect_left(ends, index)` respelling of `raw_end_of` (the review's M9b, which the
        round-5 review could not distinguish and left unpinned) fails exactly the new raw-module
        pin `test_a_group_ending_at_a_claims_end_binds_its_whole_raw_span` (two failing
        subtests). The respelling is not equivalent, so the round pins the lookup on both sides
        instead of documenting it: its `bisect_left` count drops the claim that ends exactly at
        the group's last character, and the raw end moves past the group by that claim's shift
        (the colon a render left outside goes into the digest; on a wider shape, a second
        rendered root claim behind the first, the digest binds bytes of that claim's raw span
        too).
