      needed re-rendering or rebinding; in the default scope the flagged set is the same 51
      records the head's tool flags (the documented argv/output boundary), and the round-3 rule
      changes no committed byte: every record's render is byte-identical to the pre-round-3
      head's in both scopes (and to the r1 tool's, including the two the r1 round rendered
      differently from the pre-r1 tool — `docs/evaluations/model-routing/0008b-go-bounded-regression-mixed.json`
      and its `.review.json`, where the decoded reading takes a quoted specimen's trailing
      apostrophe into the token, the fail-closed direction), so no record needed re-rendering or
      rebinding. The round-4 checks at the delivered bytes: a fresh comparison of both modules
      over all 113 records renders every record byte-identically in both scopes (0 differences;
      the round-3 bytes' own sweep result stands unchanged), and the CLI sweep above stays 0
      flagged. The r5 round's differential at its delivered bytes: the r5 sanitizer renders all
      113 records byte-identically to the r4 bytes in both scopes (0 differences), and the
      `--check --root-only` CLI sweep (one process per record) exits 0 on each of the 113 files,
      0 dirty in 49.8 s on this host, so the r5 window derivation and pins change no committed
      byte; the default-scope flagged set stays the 51 records the boundary above names. The r6
      round's sweep at its bytes: the `--check --root-only` CLI sweep (one process per record)
      exits 0 on each of the 113 files, 0 dirty in 48.5 s, and the in-process sweep renders
      every record byte-identically in the root-only scope (0 differences), the default scope
      still changing the same 51 records — the anchored read and the new pins change no
      committed byte. A seeded
      differential/invariant fuzz over random valid-JSON records (196,096
      documents, 328,604 renders; escape, mixed-spelling, nested-location, token and
      `<disposable-root>` atoms) found: every render valid JSON; every `--root-only` render a
      fixpoint except one input that is already non-idempotent at the round-3 bytes (the two
      revisions render it identically — a literal that already carries `//<disposable-root>`
      token text, whose second render binds that token's own text into a new digest; a
      pre-existing second-generation non-idempotence outside this goal's scope, reported rather
      than fixed); and four renders differing from the round-3 bytes', two inputs in both
      scopes, each the resume window's documented narrowing — a pair-free spelling deep inside
      a pair-bearing match no longer re-absorbs that match's one-escape tail, so the tail stays
      outside the token the way the pair-bearing rule spells it. The r6 round's `--root` fuzz at
      its bytes (three seeds × 20,000 records from escape, mixed-spelling, root-spelling and
      prefix atoms — 60,000 records, roots `/srv/store` and a drive-root spelling): 0
      root-only non-fixpoints; the default scope's 105 non-fixpoints all carry the disclosed
      site (a separator, literal or the `u002F` escape, directly in front of a rendered token),
      and the fragments that stay literal are spellings the families refuse by their own
      boundaries (a name-glued `srv/store`, a colon-adjacent relative spelling, a root whose
      after-lookahead refuses a name continuation), so none leaves a matched host location; a
      second run with `<disposable-root>` and `path-sha256:` atoms added gave 10 root-only
      non-fixpoints, every one an input that already spells a token (the disclosed
      hand-written-token-text class). The window's width is derived
      from the pattern, not guessed: `_DEVICE_PREFIX_WIDTH` sums the `\{1,<n>}` bounds in
      `_DEVICE_PREFIX`'s own pattern text and adds the one-byte `?`/`.` selector (the widest
      namespace, nine bytes), and `_CONTAINED_RESUME` adds the drive letter behind it, so a
      kept match's stop byte can hide a second drive location starting ten bytes before it; a
      `file:` URL's scheme colon, four bytes behind its start, is shorter. Round 4's literal
      eight hid a device-prefixed second location whose start crossed ten bytes behind the kept
      match's stop: the round-4 review's 80,000-literal fuzz with device-prefix atoms found 28
      renders differing from the round-3 bytes, all on that straddle, and zero with a ten-byte
      window, and the JSON module's straddle pin fails on windows 1, 3, 8 and 9 (the r5 round
      measured each; the derived ten passes).
      Performance, measured at the delivered bytes (minimum of five in-process runs, one module
      per process, this host; fixtures named with each number). Round 4 (the linear raw look):
      the review's UNC-listing fixture — `json.dumps({"stdout": "\n".join(...N
      `\\srv\share\dirN` paths...)})` — renders 1,000 / 2,000 / 4,000 / 8,000 / 16,000 lines in
      0.053 s / 0.150 s / 0.272 s / 0.597 s / 0.986 s root-only (base tool 0.004 s / 0.009 s /
      0.025 s / 0.036 s / 0.067 s; the r5 round re-measured this fixture's five-size series
      and the fixtures below keep the r4 round's numbers), where the round-3 bytes took
      4.6 s / 17.4 s / 101 s at 1,000 / 2,000 / 4,000
      and 433 s for the scaling pin's 8,000-line case (the review measured 3.15 s / 13.15 s /
      48.79 s on its host). One JSON literal holding 2,000 /
      8,000 space-separated escaped drive paths (`{"p":"C:` + doubled escapes + `Users` + …)
      renders in 0.092 s / 0.349 s root-only (base 0.031 s / 0.131 s; the pre-round-3 head 4.3 s
      at 2,000 and 115.5 s at 8,000, the quadratic this round's P2 fix removed); prose of 2,000 /
      8,000 lines of `line <n> wrote C:` + single-backslash `temp` + single-backslash `run<n>`
      renders in 0.123 s / 0.304 s (base 0.028 s / 0.109 s; pre-round-3 head 41.7 s at 8,000);
      the vs-base constant factor on 1 MB of inert prose is 2.8x (0.194 s -> 0.547 s) and on a
      1 MB inert literal 7.1x (0.202 s -> 1.435 s). The residual cost, measured rather than
      hidden: a `file:` URL followed by a long backslash run inside a literal still grows
      superlinearly (about 4x per doubling; the round-4 scan change does not touch it — one
      five-run series each on this host measured 0.190 s / 3.819 s at the delivered bytes
      against 0.213 s / 3.472 s at the round-3 bytes, the delivered series spread over
      3.5–5.6 s at 16,000). On the named fixture
      `{"out":"ok` + the newline escape + `file:` + N backslashes + `x"}`, root-only: base
      0.001 s / 0.004 s at N = 4,000 / 16,000, the pre-r2 tool (`f261e21`) 0.035 s / 0.554 s, the
      round-3 measurement at the delivered bytes 0.169 s / 2.692 s (default scope at 16,000:
      2.832 s; `--check --root-only`
      through the CLI 3.19 s, exit 0). The r2 raw look is where the ~5x step from the pre-r2
      tool comes from, and its guard is per literal — one escape-shaped backslash anywhere in a
      literal lets the scan run over the whole of it — so the entry's own earlier "1.04 s
      against 1.02 s" claim is superseded by these numbers (it held on a run with no
      escape-shaped backslash before it, which the guard skips).
      Round 6 re-measured the named series at its bytes in one session (minimum of three
      in-process runs, one module per process): the UNC-listing fixture 0.042 / 0.100 / 0.202 /
      0.375 / 0.753 s at 1,000 / 2,000 / 4,000 / 8,000 / 16,000 lines, the 2,000/8,000-path
      literal 0.062 / 0.262 s, the 2,000/8,000-line prose 0.057 / 0.223 s, the escape-arc
      literal 0.021 / 0.043 s, and the `file:`-run fixture 0.170 / 2.713 s at N = 4,000 /
      16,000 (the recorded 0.169 / 2.692 s re-measured within noise); the round-5 bytes measured
      in the same session gave 0.059 / 0.093 / 0.177 / 0.340 / 0.736 s on the listing, 0.061 /
      0.255 s, 0.053 / 0.222 s, 0.021 / 0.042 s and 0.176 / 2.779 s, so the anchored read
      changes no named fixture beyond host noise. The anchored pass's own adversarial shape — one
      literal of 1,000 root tokens each followed by a `//` run the family body fails — renders
      1 MB in 0.622 s against the round-5 bytes' 0.607 s, so its per-claim attempts stay linear
      in the record (the runs behind a claim reach the next claim's raw spelling, so the
      attempts' covered characters are disjoint).
      `test-model-routing-evaluation.py` was not run here (the conductor runs it; the 113-record
      sweep was 49.8 s through the CLI, one process per record, at the r5 bytes — 55-128 s
      across the r4 round's runs — host state dependent). Reader-facing boundary:
      `docs/evaluations/model-routing/README.md`; the decision, the rejected readings, the edge
      cases and the evidence plan are in
      `docs/plans/2026-11-21-sanitizer-json-escape-plan.md`.
      `check-record-cleanliness.py` is a revision-range gate and cannot run before a commit, so
      its equivalent check was run directly over this change's changed record
      (`docs/evaluations/model-routing/README.md`, `--check --root-only`) rc 0, its own suite
      passes above, and the round-2 record's `check-record-cleanliness.py --base 7a71383 --head
      bda373e` rc 0 (1 changed record passes) stays as the merged base's measurement;
      `git diff --check` rc 0. Discrimination of the round-3 pins is the mutation block above
      (the r2 round's 24-case pre-r2 measurement is superseded by the delivered suites' counts),
      and the round-3 differential fuzz is the one the evidence plan names: no invalid render,
      no non-idempotent render and no broken round trip at the delivered bytes, where the
      pre-round-3 head produced 14 invalid renders in the same corpus.

- [ ] Re-point the pre-#111 owner keys minted for recorded names that start with a colon. The
      current encoder escapes a recorded value that starts with `:` by prefixing one
      (`frontend/src/features/work/presentation.ts:94-97`), so a recorded name `:Ada` mints
      `human:::Ada` (`agent:::Ada` on the agent side), while the pre-#111 encoder spelled it
      unescaped — `human::Ada`. The reader committed with the legacy-sentinel fix
      (`ownerKeyFilter`, `:135-139`) maps only the two sentinel words, so a bookmark or hand-typed
      link carrying the pre-#111 spelling normalises to itself and matches no row, and the
      pre-#111 key for a recorded name `:unrecorded` (`human::unrecorded`) is byte-identical to
      the escape the current encoder mints for the recorded name `unrecorded`, so it silently
      selects that other row and cannot be disambiguated (the collision repeats for deeper colon
      prefixes: for a recorded name with `k >= 2` leading colons, the pre-#111 key equals the
      current mint for the same name with `k-1` leading colons, for example legacy `human:::Ada`
      (name `::Ada`) is the current mint for `:Ada`). Reproduced at `3c705d7` (production bytes
      identical to `7f62afe`) with a throwaway vitest probe over the repository's own modules
      (deleted after the run, no test file kept): rows named `:Ada`, `::Ada`, `:unrecorded` and
      `unrecorded` mint `human:::Ada`, `human::::Ada`, `human:::unrecorded` and
      `human::unrecorded`; the legacy key `human::Ada` kept its spelling and `filterWorkItems`
      selected `[]`; the legacy key `human::unrecorded` selected the row named `unrecorded`; the
      legacy key `human:::Ada` selected the row named `:Ada` instead (the deeper-prefix
      collision); and `ownerKeyLabel("human::Ada")` prints `Ada`, dropping the colon.
      Reachable: `assignee_name` and `agent_profile` are length-bounded only
      (`control/lib/symmetry_control/workspaces/schemas.ex:472-473`), so a colon-leading name can
      be served. The shape was recorded as an explicit residual of the legacy-sentinel increment
      (`docs/plans/2026-11-20-legacy-owner-bookmarks-plan.md`, "Out of scope"). Fix direction:
      decide the reading for the colon-leading pre-#111 keys (re-point them like the sentinel, or
      accept the collision and document it), then pin both shapes in
      `frontend/src/features/projects/filters.test.ts`. Suggested owner area: portal
      work-surface/frontend.

      **Accepted and documented (2026-11-21, branch `fix/colon-owner-keys`):** the repo
      owner/conductor decided, under the human owner's standing "no backward compatibility"
      directive, to accept the collision and document it; no legacy re-point is added for
      colon-leading keys — a key is always read as a current mint. The colliding pre-#111
      spellings are byte-identical to the current mints of other names (`human::unrecorded`
      for the recorded name `unrecorded`, `human:::Ada` for `:Ada`, every deeper prefix
      shifting by one), so those cannot be disambiguated. The remaining case
      (`human::Ada`, once the name `:Ada`) equals no current mint, and a re-point of only that
      unambiguous subset would add compatibility code the owner does not require. The semantics
      table, the decision and the trade-offs are in
      `docs/plans/2026-11-21-colon-owner-keys-plan.md`; the reading is stated in the
      `ownerKeyFilter` comment (`frontend/src/features/work/presentation.ts`, comment-only
      edit). Pinned by `frontend/src/features/projects/filters.test.ts`, case "reads the
      pre-#111 colon-leading owner keys as the current mints, accepting the collision":
      `human::Ada` keeps its spelling and selects no row through both reader call sites while
      `ownerKeyLabel` still renders `Ada`; `human::unrecorded` selects the row named
      `unrecorded` and not the row named `:unrecorded`; `human:::Ada` selects the row named
      `:Ada` and not the row named `::Ada`. Mutation check: re-pointing `human::Ada` to
      `human:::Ada` in `ownerKeyFilter` reddens the first pin (file `1 failed | 8 passed (9)`,
      assertion `filters.test.ts:136`, `expected { owner: 'human:::Ada' } to deeply equal
      { owner: 'human::Ada' }`); reverted byte-identically (`diff` clean), focused run back to
      `9 passed`, re-run `9 passed` after the final comment revision. Recorded runs: focused
      vitest `9 passed` exit 0; full vitest `497 passed` (20 files) exit 0 on three runs, the
      last on the final bytes (48.9s / 52.0s / 38.9s) — a first full run exited 1 with its
      failure lines not retained (the capture pipe kept only a slow-environment performance
      hint and the pnpm `[ELIFECYCLE]` line; the reruns on identical bytes are the working
      verdict and CI remains the merge gate). Gates: `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` exit 0; frontend typecheck 0,
      lint 0, build 0; `git diff --check` 0. The browser render gate was not run and the
      contract does not require it: the only production-source edit is a comment, and the built
      portal bytes are identical with the comment in and out (`index.html`
      `sha256:d2f694ddf97d861a1e00cfa577ef7ed36f0b6e2213aa4e917cb55cf52371a8e0`,
      `assets/index-Cy35eFtz.css` `sha256:0c216884aa5315566e05140b108408e7f878c0890f4c26ad6cb74794f1cafa09`,
      `assets/index-xLlmZ95U.js` `sha256:7b7562c7296d733a4c45b270be8b99f07bbf7703b147da4171730e3d73e5b133`).

## Daemon state: decorative trailing-data disjunct in the provider-action decoder (rf-w3-07 survey)

- [ ] Revalidate and merge the redundant trailing-data arms of
      `normalizeProviderActionResult` (`daemon/internal/state/provider_action.go`). At the
      rf-w3-07 base `52bdffd2` the block was
      `if token, tokenErr := decoder.Token(); tokenErr != io.EOF { if tokenErr == nil || token !=
      nil { return errors.New("provider action result has trailing data") }; return
      errors.New("provider action result has trailing data") }`: both arms returned the identical
      static error, so the disjunct could not change any outcome, and `json.Decoder.Token`
      returns a nil token whenever it returns an error. rf-w3-07 (2026-11-21) split the block to
      wrap the decoder cause (`if tokenErr != nil { wrapped }`, `if token != nil { static }`,
      final `static`) and kept the third arm because that slice's contract requires base-vs-head
      control-flow preservation for every early return; the last two arms still return the same
      bytes. No test asserts the message by equality (`grep -rn --include=*_test.go '"provider
      action result has trailing data"' daemon/` matches nothing). A follow-up maintenance task
      can collapse the arms to one static return after re-running the state suite on the Linux
      lane; the sweep and the classification table live in
      `docs/evidence/rf-w3-07-state-error-causes-2026-11-21.md` §1/§2.
      **Closed (2026-11-21, rf-w3-08 apply stage; landed on the W3-07 follow-up branch
      `refactor/rf-w3-07b-state-error-followups`, 2026-11-22):** the two identical static arms are
      collapsed to one `return nil, errors.New("provider action result has trailing data")` after
      the `tokenErr != nil` wrap, with the token value discarded (`if _, tokenErr :=
      decoder.Token(); tokenErr != io.EOF`); the wrap arm is unchanged, so the decoder cause still
      reaches callers and the static message stays byte-identical. The stale head cells are
      refreshed in the same evidence file (§2.1, §3) and the apply-stage record is its `## 12`
      section.

      **110.19 s** with (the marker path's `$RUNNER_TEMP/symmetry-linux-containment-witness/<case>/`
      prefix elided here; the CI log spells it out),
      `production_linux_containment_witness_test.go:509: wait for durable stop-receipt-before-release barrier: marker "…/lease-arm-before-resume/stop-receipt-before-release.reached" did not appear within 45s`
      — the reported `:509` is the caller frame of the `t.Helper()` runner, whose `t.Fatalf` sits at
      `daemon/e2e/production_linux_containment_witness_test.go:1026` — followed by a failure cleanup
      that could not prove the stop: `:738: Linux production witness failure cleanup could not prove
      exact helper stop/release: bound recovery did not clear journal during failure cleanup: journal
      a2c5a551-2026-45d3-9eaf-467717f281e6/1 retained containment after 45s`, `containment journal
      remains unresolved after failure cleanup: … retained containment after 5s`, and
      `failure cleanup could not prove exact helper 21107/"linux:v2:9d556fd5-837f-4845-b407-4c6aa53983b4:4026531836:21107:19582"
      gone: process 21107 with identity "…" remained live`. **Classification:** flake of the witness's
      restart replay, not a product regression — every file under `daemon/e2e`, `daemon/internal/app`
      and `daemon/internal/platform` is byte-identical between the failing revision and current main
      (`git diff --name-only 22ec193 a7afdf4 -- daemon/e2e daemon/internal/app
      daemon/internal/platform` prints nothing), the same subtest is green in `0.56 s` on main
      (`44795802448` job `136495892251`: `PASS: …/daemon_sigkill_after_lease_arm_before_resume
      (0.56s)`), the run's own merge diff (`git diff adf6d38 3b4a6ce --stat`, 14 files) touches
      neither those trees nor `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, and the failing run's uploaded evidence
      (artifact `production-linux-containment-witness-44794227912-1`, 17 files) was complete: its
      `lease-arm-before-resume.json` stops at stage
      `daemon_crashed_after_lease_arm_with_durable_mirror_seal_before_resume` while the manifest
      reports `"complete": true` and `"incomplete_evidence_files": []`, i.e. the stall left no
      daemon-side trace in that evidence at all. **Recurrence (2026-11-21):** CI run `44803673577`
      attempt 1 (PR #126 head `17d838b`, the merge of `origin/main` into
      `fix/provider-action-chain-removal`) failed the same subtest in its `integration` job
      (`136524113298`) after **110.29 s**, with the identical
      `production_linux_containment_witness_test.go:509` 45 s barrier line and the same cleanup
      shape (journal `b2090059-1137-4b6b-a975-58f1e202834c/1` retained containment after 45s;
      helper 15580 remained live), while main's last six CI runs (through `44804764621` @
      `ea65742`) were all green. **Suspect path** for the restart pass:
      `daemon/internal/app/cleanup_glue.go:118-143` (record the durable receipt →
      `verifyDurableContainmentStopReceipt` at `:285` → `observeContainmentStop`) →
      `waitForLinuxStopReceiptBarrier` (`:203`), which publishes the reached marker at `:234` only
      after both marker paths pass `assertLinuxStopReceiptBarrierPathAbsent` (`:218`/`:221`), and
      whose own release bound is `linuxStopReceiptBarrierTimeout = 30s` (`:21`); the marker never
      appeared, so either the restart pass never reached the callback or it stalled before
      publishing. **Diagnosis rule:** read the restarted daemon's log tail now exported by
      `scripts/ci/export-linux-v2-redacted-evidence.py` (2026-11-21, `witness-stall-evidence`): a
      failed legacy witness run adds `<case>-second-daemon-stdout.json` and `-stderr.json` to the
      `production-linux-containment-witness-<run id>-<attempt>` artifact, carrying a ≤64 KiB
      sanitized tail of the `second-daemon.*.log` files written at
      `daemon/e2e/production_linux_containment_witness_test.go:1723-1754`; each record keeps
      `time`/`level` plus the allowlisted message/enum/integer fields, while identities, paths and
      `error` text are intentionally never exported and `dropped_lines` counts lines that could not
      be projected (a Go panic dump therefore appears only as a count). The 45 s harness wait and the
      30 s daemon bound are declared bounds, not the defect: no timeout changes until an occurrence
      has been diagnosed from that tail.

      **Closed (2026-11-21, branch `fix/witness-owner-loss-latch`, fix commit `e9c457d8`):** the
      next occurrence was diagnosed from the tail this entry's diagnosis rule asked for — CI run
      `44876100236` attempt 1 (PR #168 head `bb26602`, merge `680a2085` on base `6a815c0f`; artifact
      `production-linux-containment-witness-44876100236-1`, whose
      `lease-arm-before-resume-second-daemon-stdout.json` carries 18 ×
      `stop_recovered_process_failed` — first 7.8 ms after `runtime_registered`, then a 29.999 s gap
      matching the persisted 30 s lease, then a 1 Hz train — stderr empty, 0 dropped lines; frozen
      journal `has_stop_receipt:false`, helper scan `completed_sequence:1`, mirror sealed 2, target
      gone, `containment_unproven:false`, helper alive across daemon restarts). Root cause
      confirmed: the helper processed the control-pipe EOF before the owner watchdog published
      owner loss, so the first `stop()` took the live-owner branch; its final descendant scan
      persisted a `begin` fence over the helper→daemon metadata transport that died with the
      SIGKILLed daemon, the failed persist latched sticky descendant uncertainty, and every
      recovery stop was refused (`Linux descendant containment is unproven`), so the receipt
      callback / 45 s harness barrier (`stop-receipt-before-release.reached`) was never reached,
      and the failure cleanup's third daemon failed identically for 45 s. Fix: `enterRecovery`
      (`daemon/internal/platform/supervisor_linux_helper.go`) now latches owner loss at entry — the
      watchdog arm, control EOF/error, and failed response writes all latch it — so the first stop
      uses the observer-free owner-loss branch and seals descendant scans locally. Two of those
      entries can be reached while the owner process is still alive (the daemon closes its own
      control writer on response-transport loss, and a response write can fail before any pipe
      I/O); for them the helper seals locally with no observer writes, the restarted-daemon
      consumer records the returned seal durably (`RecordSupervisorRecoveryScanSeal`, Completed+1)
      before the receipt, and the in-process response-loss consumer does not yet, so that stop
      fails closed into a durable containment-unproven mark — pinned by
      `TestLinuxSupervisorLiveOwnerControlCloseInProcessStopFailsClosed`, follow-up tracked in
      `docs/TODO.md` (PR #185 review round 1, finding F1). The artifact's missing
      attribution is closed too: `stop_recovered_process_failed` now logs its already-computed
      `reason` (one line, `daemon/internal/app/recovery_glue.go`; every value is already in the
      evidence exporter's `reason` allowlist and the exporter suite stays green). Pinned by the
      deterministic platform regression test
      `TestLinuxSupervisorControlEOFWithDeadOwnerMetadataUsesOwnerLossSeal`
      (`daemon/internal/platform/containment_supervisor_linux_test.go`), which constructs the
      ordering itself (daemon-side metadata connection closed before the control pipe, owner writer
      left open): on the unfixed tree it fails 20/20 through the same sticky `descendantScanLost`
      latch and `ErrLinuxDescendantContainmentUnproven` class — with the owner writer open the
      message is `Linux descendant containment is unproven: descendant observation was uncertain`,
      and once the owner pipe closes it reads `owner loss interrupted descendant observation`,
      which is what the CI retries would have produced (the CI error text itself was never
      exported); with the fix it passes 50/50 and mints a receipt with `sealSequence == 2`
      (previous clean sequence 1 + 1). Residuals, recorded in the plan and tracked in `docs/TODO.md`
      rather than left implicit: the exact originating write-error suffix (broken pipe vs
      acknowledgement EOF) was never exported (the next failing artifact should cross-check
      `reason` against the refusal; the fix removes the dead-transport branch for either suffix);
      the lease-expiry arm still stays on the live-owner branch, where the same sticky refusal
      class remains reachable if the owner dies or wedges as the lease expires (root-cause §5 P2
      direction); and the live-owner entries above still lack an in-process consumer for the local
      seal. Plan and gate evidence:
      `docs/plans/2026-11-21-witness-owner-loss-latch-plan.md`. The 45 s harness wait and the 30 s
      daemon bound were not changed.

## CI Go toolchain: bump past the stdlib advisories

- [ ] Bump the CI-pinned Go toolchain past the 12 standard-library vulnerabilities the advisory
      govulncheck step reports. `go-version: 1.27.0` is pinned in six places in
      `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and once in `.github/workflows/linux-native-v2-acceptance.yml`;
      hosted CI run 44862556617's `daemon` job shows `Go: go1.27.0`,
      `govulncheck_exit_status=3`, `govulncheck_vulnerabilities=12`, every finding fixed in
      go1.27.2-go1.27.4 (for example GO-2026-6859 `net/http@go1.27.4`, GO-2026-6809
      `crypto/x509@go1.27.3`), while the same command on a go1.27.4 host reports none.
      Found 2026-11-21 by refactor slice W1-01 (branch `refactor/rf-w1-01-ci-quality-plane`,
      plan `docs/plans/2026-11-21-ci-quality-plane-plan.md`), which records the 12 as the
      advisory baseline and leaves the pin alone: changing it alters pre-existing steps, which
      the slice's contract (clause 3) rules out. Fix direction: raise `go-version` in every
      workflow that sets it, re-run the advisory step and record the patched-toolchain count.
      Owner area: CI tooling.

      **Closed (2026-11-22, branch `refactor/ci-toolchain-bump`, fix commit `385070f1`, plan
      `docs/plans/2026-11-22-ci-toolchain-bump-plan.md`):** `go-version` is `1.27.4` at all eight
      sites - seven in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (`:208`, `:912`, `:1060`, `:1504`, `:1933`,
      `:2132` and the quoted `'1.27.4'` at `:2413`) and the quoted one at
      `.github/workflows/linux-native-v2-acceptance.yml:67` - and the same two workflows took
      `actions/checkout` 4 to 7 (16 sites), `actions/setup-go` 5 to 7 (8 sites) and
      `actions/download-artifact` 4 to 8 (5 sites). No step, input, `if:` or `needs:` changed: each
      intervening major's documented breaking change is analysed against this repository's usage in
      the plan (download-artifact v5's by-ID path fix and v8's `digest-mismatch: error` default and
      non-zipped-download handling; setup-go v6's `GOTOOLCHAIN=local` toolchain handling and
      go.mod-based default cache key; checkout v6's separate credentials file and v7's fork-PR
      refusal for `pull_request_target`/`workflow_run`), and no site uses the inputs, ids or triggers
      those changes act on. The entry's "six places in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`" was one behind the
      tree: the re-derived count at the baseline `12de9428` is seven, the `compose` job's site being
      the seventh. Measured from `daemon/` with the `daemon` job's own scanner and counting
      expression (`govulncheck` v1.10.0, `vuln.go.dev` snapshot 2026-11-11, `GOOS=linux
      GOARCH=amd64`, `./...`, `grep -c '^Vulnerability #'`): at go1.27.0 exit 3 with
      `govulncheck_vulnerabilities=12`, reproducing the hosted baseline of run 44862556617's `daemon`
      job (GO-2026-6859 `net/http` fixed in go1.27.4, GO-2026-6809 `crypto/x509` fixed in go1.27.3,
      the other ten fixed in go1.27.2); at go1.27.4 exit 0 with `govulncheck_vulnerabilities=0`
      (`No vulnerabilities found.`). Hosted confirmation: PR #218 run `44959141686`, `daemon` job
      logged `Go: go1.27.4`, `No vulnerabilities found.` and `govulncheck_vulnerabilities=0`.
      Dependabot #168 (`actions/setup-go` 5 to 7) and #171 (`actions/checkout` 4 to 7) are
      superseded by this change and are closed when it merges.

      `required-version = "==0.17.5"`, so any other ruff refuses to load the configuration
      (exit 2, `Required version … does not match the running version …`) instead of reporting a
      different finding set than the one the 2026-11-21 baseline fixed (373 findings at
      `a3039f0`, of which 220 E501 at `line-length = 120`).

      (re-derived from the released `ruff-x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu.tar.gz` on 2026-11-22), so the

      `ruff format --check scripts/ci` → exit 0, `58 files already formatted`. The other half of
      this entry, the `extend-exclude` block, stays open in `docs/TODO.md`.

## scripts/ci: install a pinned PyYAML for the lockfile differential oracle (W6-01)

- [ ] The `ci.yml` `daemon` job's "Check CI gate helper scripts" step
      (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:1482-1493` as of `6a815c0f`, which runs
      `python3 ../scripts/ci/test-check-lockfile-registry.py` at `:1489`) must install a pinned
      PyYAML and export `SYMMETRY_REQUIRE_PYYAML=1` for that step, so the differential cross-check
      between `check-lockfile-registry.py`'s own YAML reader and PyYAML can never silently vanish
      again. This is the recorded follow-up of contract `goal-rf-w3-16-scripts-defects` (refactor
      wave 3, SC S8, branch `refactor/rf-w3-16-scripts-defects`, plan
      `docs/plans/2026-11-21-rf-w3-16-scripts-defects-plan.md`): the suite now prints the exact
      notice `PyYAML differential oracle skipped: module not importable` on stderr and asserts
      that string when the module is not importable, and with `SYMMETRY_REQUIRE_PYYAML=1` set the
      differential test fails instead of skipping. The repository still declares no Python
      dependency and no workflow performs a `pip install`, so on a runner without PyYAML the
      oracle stays skipped. Measured 2026-11-21 at `a7681da4` (this slice's increment commit on top
      of `36f9a887`): portable Python 3.12.10 on the Windows host has PyYAML 6.0.3 and runs `Ran 24
      tests`, `OK` with no skip; WSL Ubuntu-24.04 Python 3.12.3 has PyYAML 6.0.1 and is `OK`
      likewise; `python -S` (site-packages off) and `python3 -S` print the notice and report `OK
      (skipped=1)`; `SYMMETRY_REQUIRE_PYYAML=1
      python -S scripts/ci/test-check-lockfile-registry.py` exits 1 with `FAILED (failures=1)` on
      both platforms. Pin the version next to the other tool pins in `ci.yml` (the W1-01 ruff-pin
      entry's `required-version = "==0.17.5"` handshake at `docs/TODO.md:744` is the same
      pattern) and set the environment variable in the same step, so a lost install reddens the
      job instead of shrinking the oracle. `ci.yml` is untouched by rf-w3-16 because W3-01/#157
      owned it in wave 3 (that PR has since landed as `6a815c0f`, the revision the line numbers
      above bind); W6-01 is its wave-6 owner.

      **Closed (2026-11-22, branch `refactor/rf-w6-01-ci-structure-finalize`):** the step now
      installs the pin itself (`pyyaml_version='6.0.3'`,
      `python3 -m pip install --user --break-system-packages --disable-pip-version-check --quiet
      "PyYAML==$pyyaml_version"`), asserts `yaml.__version__` equals it, sets
      `SYMMETRY_REQUIRE_PYYAML: "1"` as the step's env, and runs the suite in the same step.
      `--break-system-packages` is what the ubuntu runner's PEP 668 externally-managed Python
      requires ([actions/runner-images#10897](https://github.com/actions/runner-images/issues/10897));
      `--user` keeps the install out of the apt-managed `dist-packages`. Measured on the Windows
      host (PyYAML 6.0.3): `SYMMETRY_REQUIRE_PYYAML=1 python3 scripts/ci/test-check-lockfile-registry.py`
      → exit 0, `Ran 25 tests`, `OK`, no skip; `python3 -S scripts/ci/test-check-lockfile-registry.py`
      → exit 0, `OK (skipped=1)` with one `PyYAML differential oracle skipped: module not
      importable` notice; `SYMMETRY_REQUIRE_PYYAML=1 python3 -S
      scripts/ci/test-check-lockfile-registry.py` → exit 1, `FAILED (failures=1)` on
      `SYMMETRY_REQUIRE_PYYAML=1 requires the PyYAML differential oracle, and it is not importable`.
      The pinned download itself runs only on the CI runner, recorded as such.

## Comment-policy sweep: debt the deleted comments recorded (2026-11-22)

- [ ] `frontend/src/services/api.ts`: Goal command/creation writes have no caller-cancellation
      signal — the W4-09 reads/writes split owns adding one; `run()` accepts an optional `signal`
      the write path never supplies.

      **Closed (2026-11-22, branch `refactor/rf-w4-09-api-reads-writes-split`, plan
      `docs/plans/2026-11-22-rf-w4-09-api-reads-writes-split-plan.md`):** declined, not added, by
      the slice's binding conductor decision option (a). A write keeps no signal: a hidden write
      deadline would be new production behaviour with no accepted requirement, and D1 freezes the
      wire bytes, so the split states the decision in the type surface instead — `Writes` has no
      signal parameter, and a caller cannot believe a command write is cancellable. The Goal
      command/creation path's source (`postGoalCommand`/`postGoalCreation` through `goalCommand`)
      is unchanged; like every write, its fetch init no longer carries the runtime's
      never-aborted step signal, and `run()`'s optional `signal` remains for the read half only.

- [ ] `daemon/internal/platform/supervisor_linux_helper.go`: the in-process consumer of
      `recoverResponseLossStop` does not record the helper's authenticated recovery scan seal, so
      such a stop fails closed into a durable containment-unproven mark; only the restarted-daemon
      path records it (`TestLinuxSupervisorLiveOwnerControlCloseInProcessStopFailsClosed` pins the
      behaviour, not the missing seal record).

      **Closed (2026-11-23, branch `refactor/rf-w4-22-response-loss-seal-consumer`):** the same
      change pays this debt. The in-process consumer now records the authenticated seal before the
      receipt, and the pin that recorded the missing-seal behaviour asserts the durable seal under
      its new name (`…StopRecordsSeal`). The entry's file reference is re-derived: the helper loop
      and `enterRecovery` live in `daemon/internal/platform/supervisor_session.go` at this base, and
      their surviving two-line comment needed no edit — the pre-slice #185 comment that named the
      deferral was shortened by this sweep before the slice started. Evidence record:
      `docs/evidence/rf-w4-22-response-loss-seal-consumer-2026-11-23.md`.

- [ ] `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: Credo printed the same numbered list for files skipped over
      `parse_timeout` and for unparsable files, so the strict baseline could deflate while only a
      guard printed.

      **Closed (2026-11-23, branch `refactor/rf-w6-10-elixir-lint-coverage-flips`, plan
      `docs/plans/2026-11-23-rf-w6-10-elixir-lint-coverage-flips-plan.md`):** the Credo step is the
      blocking ratchet gate, and `scripts/ci/assert-credo-strict-baseline.py` refuses a report whose
      analyzed source set was skipped — a skipped file now fails the gate instead of printing a
      guard. Credo keeps printing one numbered list for both causes; that is a limit of its output,
      not a hole in the gate. The actionlint half of the item stays open in `docs/TODO.md`.

## Codex adapter giant-file split (rf-w5-16, 2026-11-23)

      named transitions and one test-only violation hook), `rpc_session.go` (886 L: the

      (two disclosed order deviations inside one uninterrupted critical section, plan section 11)

      distinct values (391 — occurrence counts drop only where the dedupe keys were single-sourced,

      mutants. The fix rounds hardened the owner-file write scan: map deletion, `clear` and
      pointer-alias writes are refused (plan section 18, M8-M10), and a `cancelTracker` method

## Linux supervisor: remaining sticky-refusal paths after the owner-loss latch

- [ ] Give the in-process `recoverResponseLossStop` consumer the helper's owner-loss terminal seal.
      The live-owner entries of the latch (a daemon-side control close on response-transport loss,
      and response writes that fail before any pipe I/O) make the helper seal locally with no
      observer writes, so the durable helper progress keeps its pre-latch sequence; the restarted
      daemon records the seal returned over the recovery endpoint
      (`RecordSupervisorRecoveryScanSeal`, exact `Completed+1`) before the receipt, but the
      in-process consumer does not, `assert_all_sealed` refuses, and that stop fails closed into a
      durable containment-unproven mark. Pinned by
      `TestLinuxSupervisorLiveOwnerControlCloseInProcessStopFailsClosed`; the amendment (record the
      returned seal before the mirror freeze, added once where the run-journal callback is plumbed)
      is deferred to the RunJournalRecorder lane — the callback is added once there instead of being
      hand-copied into every adapter — and the interim state stays fail-closed (PR #185 review
      round 1, finding F1; conductor decision 2026-11-21).

      **Closed (2026-11-23, branch `refactor/rf-w4-22-response-loss-seal-consumer`, base
      `e13450bc`, plan `docs/plans/2026-11-23-rf-w4-22-response-loss-seal-consumer-plan.md`):** the
      consumer records the helper's returned seal through one new `execution.Recorder` method,
      `RecordSupervisorRecoveryScanSeal` (the port's eleventh member), implemented once in the app
      by the restart consumer's own `daemon.persistSupervisorRecoveryScanSeal` (exact `Completed+1`,
      one exact replay, plus the store-lifetime guard) and plumbed app → execution → platform as
      `LinuxSupervisorStartSpec.RecordRecoveryScanSeal`; no adapter hand-copies it and the ten
      frozen OpenCode mirrors stay ten. The write sits after the mirror's durable seal and before
      the durable `assert_all_sealed` assertion — the literal "before the mirror freeze" is
      infeasible because the store refuses a helper recovery seal without a durable mirror seal
      (`daemon/internal/state/containment_journal.go:413-415`) and the mirror seal is written by the
      freeze itself; the amendment is recorded in the plan §3.1 — and still before the receipt
      adoption. The pin is renamed
      `TestLinuxSupervisorLiveOwnerControlCloseInProcessStopFailsClosed` →
      `TestLinuxSupervisorLiveOwnerControlCloseInProcessStopRecordsSeal` and asserts the durable
      state (helper `completed=2, sealed=2`, mirror `2/2`, one accepted assertion, one seal write),
      the ordered tail `mirror:assert_clean:2, mirror:seal:2, recovery-scan-seal:2,
      mirror:assert_all_sealed:2` and the adopted receipt; three further arms pin a failing seal
      write (fail-closed, one mark, no durable advance, the unchanged wrapper and sentinels), the
      absent write (the byte-identical base refusal with its exact replay) and a resumed helper's
      non-2 terminal seal (the recorded sequence is the helper's attested one, not a constant). The
      launch carrier is pinned by `TestLinuxSupervisorStartSpecCarriesEveryLaunchWrite`. Measured
      WSL durable order: base refuses `assert_all_sealed` twice and adopts no receipt; HEAD writes
      exactly one `recovery-scan-seal:2` at that position and completes. Mutants killed on WSL
      (evidence record §6): no write, write after the mirror freeze, `Completed+2`, swallowed write
      error, constant `2`, and a nil launcher carrier. Gates: `go vet ./...` exit 0; `GOOS=linux`,
      `GOOS=windows` and `GOOS=darwin` `go build ./...` exit 0; Windows `go test -count=1
      ./internal/execution/... ./internal/platform/...` exit 0 (execution, platform) and
      `./internal/app/...` exit 0 (app, apptest); WSL `go test -count=1 -race
      ./internal/platform/... ./internal/app/... ./internal/execution/...` exit 0; `python3
      scripts/ci/test-omo-control-native-evidence.py` exit 0. Residuals stay tracked: a
      recorder-less OpenCode launch keeps the fail-closed stop until W6-04 (the port entry above),
      and the lease-expiry arm stays open in `docs/TODO.md`.

## Linux owner preflight: delegated `manager/cgroup.kill` root-only refusal under systemd ≥254

Recorded by goal `rf-acc-native-hosted-run` (2026-11-23): on the hosted `ubuntu-latest` runner
(systemd 259), the installed daemon's `-verify-linux-owner-install` ExecStartPre refused at the
delegated `DelegateSubgroup=manager` subgroup's `cgroup.kill`, which systemd chowns to the service
user (probe run `45169655828`: `55200:55200 200` and that user can open it for writing; in-guest
diagnostic in run `45169868354`: `52000:52000 200` while the unit's own `cgroup.kill` stays
`0:0 200`; refusal recorded in run `45164286155`). Making a credentialed run pass that step needed
a production-side decision (installer re-chown once the subgroup exists, a reviewed preflight
change, or a host whose systemd keeps the control root-owned). The entry text landed on main as
`dd758599` (#270, `refactor/rf-acc-native-hosted-run`) while this slice was in flight; it is not in
`docs/TODO.md` at `b9d4b4a4`.

**Closed (2026-11-23, branch `refactor/rf-acc-delegated-cgroup-kill`, base `b9d4b4a4`, plan
`docs/plans/2026-11-23-rf-acc-delegated-cgroup-kill-plan.md`, record
`docs/evidence/rf-acc-delegated-cgroup-kill-2026-11-23.md`):** `verifyLinuxOwnerParentKillBoundary`
(`daemon/internal/linuxpreflight/cgroup_linux.go`) no longer requires the delegated
`manager/cgroup.kill` to be root-owned and A-unwritable — no stock systemd ≥254 host can satisfy
that inside the delegated subtree — and enforces the retained boundary one level up instead: the
owning unit cgroup's own `cgroup.kill` must be root:root, a plain owner-writable file with no
group/other write, and refused for an A `O_WRONLY` open (ENOENT is a failure). The manager file
must still be a plain file owned by root or A with a gid matching its owner, owner-writable and
with no group/other write, so B cannot write it. Local WSL2 systemd 255 measurement (`systemctl --version`
`255.4-1ubuntu8.17`; real v2 owner install; mandated unit) reproduced the hosted ownership at all
three phases (after start, after `daemon-reload`, after restart): `manager/cgroup.kill`
`1002:1003 0200` and A-writable, unit `cgroup.kill` `0:0 0200` with `EACCES` for A — which also
settles the older 0006 design record's ":613-617" claim that a systemd 255 host keeps the manager
file root-owned (not reproduced; the record now carries an appendix). Live rows on that host, the
built daemon run as A: base `b9d4b4a4` exits 1 (`manager control cgroup.kill is not
owned/writable only by its trusted owner`), HEAD exits 0, the unit kill temporarily chowned to A
(root) exits 1 (`unit cgroup.kill must remain root:root`), and a retarget mutant that points the
unit check at `manager/cgroup.kill` exits 1 on the same host. Unit rows pin the acceptance set per
check: root-owned is accepted by both checks and A-owned only by the manager file, while the unit
file must remain root:root; both checks reject B-owned, wrong-owner-gid, group-writable,
other-writable, owner-unwritable and non-plain-file rows; mutants M1 (accept a B-owned manager
kill), M2 (drop the unit-kill root check) and M4 (accept a group-writable manager kill) each
redden `TestLinuxOwnerKillBoundaryMetadata` and were reverted (`sha256sum` restored), and the M3
union mutant (accept an A-writable unit kill: unit metadata root check and A-write refusal
removed) is red in the new hermetic `TestLinuxOwnerUnitKillWriteProbeDecisions` and
`TestLinuxOwnerUnitKillControlReadsTheDerivedUnitPath` rows as well as on the live chowned host,
where it accepts with exit 0 while the fixed code refuses. Gates: `gofmt -l .` exit 0;
`go vet ./internal/linuxpreflight/...` exit 0 (Windows and WSL); `GOOS=linux go build ./...` exit
0; WSL `go test -race -count=1 ./internal/linuxpreflight/...` exit 0. Residual: the A `O_WRONLY`
probe's independent discriminating power (a root-owned unit kill A could still write through a
capability) was never demonstrated live — a capable A client is refused earlier during anchor
discovery (`sendmsg: broken pipe`; the refusing check was not identified, record sections 9 and
11) — so on every known host state the probe is defense-in-depth beyond the metadata rule, and its
decisions and derived-path wiring are now covered hermetically (record section 13); and the
rf-acc-native-hosted-run paragraph, landed with #270 while this slice was in flight, records the
decision as open while this entry and the goal record carry the closure.

## Deferred review findings (PR #205)

- [ ] STD-A09 from the Opus review of PR #205 (2026-11-21, branch
      `refactor/rf-w4-14-browser-fixtures`): the merged #188 test-observability code loses the
      `(partial)` label on a timeout-truncated package once any test in that package failed.
      `scripts/ci/go-test-durations.py:213` computes
      `unexplained = [package for package in failed_packages if not any(key[0] == package for key in failed)]`,
      so a package that has a failed test counts as fully explained even when its stream is cut
      off by a timeout before the remaining tests ran. The step is `continue-on-error` today, so
      there is no gate impact, but the W2-01/W6-12 follow-up that flips the thresholds to blocking
      must fix the partial-run detection first.

      **Closed (2026-11-24, branch `refactor/rf-w6-12-wall-clock-ratchets`, plan
      `docs/plans/2026-11-24-rf-w6-12-wall-clock-ratchets-plan.md`):** the summarizer now counts a
      test whose `run` event has no terminal `pass`/`fail`/`skip` event and refuses the stream
      (exit 2) with the unfinished tests named — a package that carries a named failed test *and*
      a cut-off stream is no longer read as explained. The flip the entry guarded landed in the
      same change: the duration step is blocking, and
      `scripts/ci/test-go-test-durations.py` pins the refusal on the exact stream shape
      (one failed test, one unfinished test, a package-level failure).

## Descendant window under host load (rf-flake-stdout-drain-64kib)

- [ ] The descendant window under host load (found by the goal's gate runs; brought into scope and
      fixed by the 2026-11-24 ruling). `daemon/internal/execution/runner_test.go`'s helper mode
      `root-exits-child-holding-stdout` spawns a grandchild, sleeps a fixed 100 ms and exits; the
      runner tears the containment tree down on root exit, so the grandchild's first stdout write is
      delivered only inside that window. Under this host's load the grandchild's own spawn+exit wall
      time is 109–756 ms (median 131 ms plain, 134 ms instrumented), and
      `TestRunBoundedCommandStopsDescendantHoldingOutputPipe`
      (`probe_test.go:83`, empty output) and
      `TestRootExitDoesNotLeaveADescendantHoldingStdoutOnWindows` (`runner_windows_test.go:218`, the
      30 s hang guard) red 7–8 of 20 focused iterations **without** `-coverprofile` and on the
      pre-change tree alike. Fix shape: a readiness handshake (the root waits for the grandchild's
      first write or a marker instead of sleeping), which the rf-w6-12 census had already deferred as
      a new seam (`docs/plans/2026-11-24-rf-w6-12-wall-clock-ratchets-plan.md` §4 G3); a timeout raise
      is not the route (the w6-12 slice and this contract both refuse it). Candidate row for the
      conductor's known-flakes ledger (its `daemon` `TestStartDrains…` entry is a different,
      now-resolved signature). Owner area: daemon execution test harness.

      **Closed (2026-11-24, branch `refactor/rf-flake-stdout-drain-64kib`, base `9d113eb6`, plan
      `docs/plans/2026-11-24-rf-flake-stdout-drain-64kib-plan.md`):** the conductor ruling of
      2026-11-24 brought the item into scope for the goal that found it. The helper mode now waits for
      a readiness marker instead of sleeping: the grandchild creates the marker (path from the
      `GO_RUNNER_DESCENDANT_MARKER` env the test supplies) immediately after its first stdout write,
      and the root polls for the marker (1 ms interval, bounded by `testHangGuard`) and exits only
      then — so the write can no longer land after the containment teardown. All three call sites
      (`probe_test.go:76`, `runner_unix_test.go:50`, `runner_windows_test.go:211`) supply the marker
      path via `descendantHoldingStdoutInvocation`; `minimalEnvironment`, the payload assertions and
      `ci.yml` are untouched; no timeout was raised, retried or skipped. Red-without / green-with
      evidence, the mutant row that restores the fixed 100 ms sleep, and the coverage-mode repeats:
      `docs/evidence/rf-flake-stdout-drain-64kib-2026-11-24.md`.

## Installed-daemon smoke owner check (rf-acc-native-burndown, 2026-11-24)

- [ ] Decide the product-side fix for the installed-daemon smoke's first failing check (2026-11-24,
      goal `rf-acc-native-burndown`; evidence
      `docs/evidence/rf-acc-native-burndown-2026-11-24.md`): `symmetry-daemon.service`'s own
      `ExecStartPre` (`/usr/libexec/symmetry/symmetry-daemon -verify-linux-owner-install`) failed
      deterministically with `effective symmetry-owner.service unit violates the owner service
      contract: effective owner unit depends on or orders against symmetry-daemon.service through
      Before` — the owner unit's effective `Before` contains `symmetry-daemon.service` because
      systemd registers the inverse of the daemon unit's own required
      `After=symmetry-owner.service` (`scripts/systemd/symmetry-daemon.service.in:3`) on the owner,
      and `daemon/internal/linuxpreflight/systemd_linux.go:233` rejected that inverse entry, while
      the owner fragment stays forbidden to declare `Before=`/an edge to the daemon
      (`systemd_linux.go:498` `rejectLinuxSystemdUnitLists`, `systemd_linux.go:773-784`
      `validateLinuxSystemdOverrides`).

      **Closed (2026-11-24, branch `refactor/rf-acc-native-burndown`, base `668e75de`):** the
      conductor ruled the check broken and its fix in scope. `Before` is removed from the owner
      effective-key list (`systemd_linux.go:235`); `After`, `BindsTo`, `Requires`, `Requisite`,
      `Wants`, `PartOf` and `Conflicts` stay refused. Tests kept in
      `daemon/internal/linuxpreflight/preflight_linux_test.go`: an effective owner
      `Before=symmetry-daemon.service` passes (`:870-876`), each remaining key reddens its own
      `owner/<key>` row (`:877-889`), and a declared `before` in the owner fragment (`:525-527`) or
      drop-in (`:539-541`) is still refused. Mutants: re-adding `Before` reddens the accept test;
      dropping `After` reddens `owner/After` (record §6). The class-E half of the original item
      stayed open and moved to `docs/TODO.md` under `## CI consolidation owner decisions
      (2026-11-17)` as the Control assignment product question; the OpenCode `TestedVersion` pin
      staleness stays `F2-excluded` per `refactor/decision-native-opencode-2026-11-24.md`.

## Daemon: the `admission_deadline` attach-ack subtest flakes on its wall-clock deadline (PR #217 review)

- [ ] Revalidate and diagnose the pre-existing flake in
      `TestOmOAttachAckRegressionCancellationAndDeadlineDoNotRenewBudget/admission_deadline`
      (`daemon/internal/app/omo_attach_ack_test.go`; the `admission_deadline` arm sets a 2 s
      wall-clock admission `DeadlineAt` and then requires a loopback GET+PUT inside it). The PR #217
      review observed it fail twice on `394859a9` (once under concurrent load, once alone) with
      `assertRequests`'s `attachment requests=[] … context deadline exceeded`, then pass 2/2 alone
      and 3/3 on the PR head. Failures take one of two shapes: the attach GET leg hits the deadline
      (`native_calls=[start details open details close wait]`), or the admission check fires first
      (`Goal admission deadline has elapsed before native launch`, `resolveGoalAdapter` in
      `goal_admission_tx.go`); both are recorded under load in
      `docs/evidence/rf-w4-03-runner-decomposition-2026-11-21.md`
      (the app-suite failure paragraph). The failure is consistent with the deadline expiring when
      the suite is starved, but the cause is unconfirmed. The arm's duration is bounded by a
      production limit, so a fix needs an owner decision on the deadline or the assertion;
      revalidate against a recovered failure before changing either.

      **Closed (2026-11-24, branch `refactor/rf-flake-omo-attach-ack-deadline`, plan
      `docs/plans/2026-11-24-rf-flake-omo-attach-ack-deadline-plan.md`):** the revalidation
      recovered 22 reds in a 200-iteration loaded repeat at the base revision (21 starved-A, 1
      in-flight readback) with monotonic stage marks in the fixture: every red's budget died in the
      post-claim pipeline (the one boundary red reached the first served request at 1.551 s of the
      2 s budget and had its PUT refused 0.955 s past the deadline; the rest never reached the
      server), confirming a host wall-clock miss rather than a product defect. The arm now anchors
      `Limits.DeadlineAt` inside the fixture's `Claim` — where the product parses the claim's
      `Work` (`daemon/internal/app/app.go:1359`) — and accepts exactly the fail-closed trajectories
      the probe and pins recover (intended, late-served, starved-A, in-flight readback, starved-B),
      each strictly asserted; a third request, a post-expiry readback, a turn start, a changed
      journal or an unaccounted `[GET]` stays red. No budget raise, retry, skip or removed
      assertion. Evidence: `docs/evidence/rf-flake-omo-attach-ack-deadline-2026-11-24.md` — loaded
      repeat 22 red/200 before → 0 red/200 after, companion 0 red/200 with the trajectory
      histogram, the pins and mutant rows with restored blob hashes, WSL `-race` exit 0. Residual:
      a further fail-closed micro-shape (for example a readback aborted by the budget with no PUT
      attempt) would still red and needs the same treatment as the recovered families.
      CI `daemon`/`daemon-windows` on the PR head: pending (conductor-owned).

## Observed flakes, cause unknown: three observations (2026-11-22 CI runs and PR #220 review)

- [ ] `TestAutonomousReturnReleasesItsInputReader` (`daemon/cmd/symmetry-fake-agent/autonomous_test.go`;
      package `daemon/cmd/symmetry-fake-agent`): failed once in the hosted `daemon` job's race step
      (`go test -race -count=1 -timeout 600s -json ./...` through `scripts/ci/stdio-only-caller-boundary.py`),
      run `44976804508`, on PR #225 head `d45dc45f` — a Control-only code change
      (`control/lib/symmetry_control_web/protocol.ex` plus its reason-coverage test and two docs records;
      `git diff --name-only origin/main...d45dc45f`), so the daemon tree is untouched by that PR. Observed
      failure text: the test's own `t.Fatalf("reader goroutine is still alive when runAutonomous returned")`
      with the goroutine dump it printed — `goroutine 22 [runnable]` in `runAutonomous.func1` (the reader
      closure in `autonomous.go`, reported at the function's tail, with no `readLine` or select frame),
      `created by ... runAutonomous in goroutine 50`. Reproduction
      attempts and outcomes: the same test passed on the PR's previous and next heads (CI reruns), and no
      other occurrence is recorded in the repository; added 2026-11-22 — Linux (WSL, go1.27.0) `go test
      -race -count=20 -timeout 600s -v -run '^TestAutonomousReturnReleasesItsInputReader$'
      ./cmd/symmetry-fake-agent/` → 20/20 `--- PASS`, exit 0. Cause unknown. Most probable mechanism
      (inference, re-derived from this run's own `-json` record): the input is a closable `io.Pipe` whose
      `Close` does unblock the parked `readLine`, so the reader's exit is reachable; the record's
      `"Elapsed":0.01` (`--- FAIL: ... (0.01s)`) is below `runAutonomous`'s `readerJoinTimeout = 50 ms` at
      that head, so the join's `time.After(readerJoinTimeout)` arm cannot have fired — the run returned
      through `<-readerDone`, so the reader had already run its deferred `close(readerDone)` and was in its
      exit epilogue when the scan ran, and `bytes.Contains(stacks, "runAutonomous.func1")` reads that
      exiting goroutine as a live leak. Next diagnostic step: repeat the `-race -count=N` run under CPU
      contention, and on a reproduction capture whether the reader's `close(readerDone)` had already run
      when the scan executed (the goleak oracle in the same file is the existing alternative oracle) rather
      than touching the assertion or the bound, which the sibling
      `TestAutonomousReturnBoundsTheJoinOnAnUnblockableCarrier` pins (`--- PASS: ... (0.05s)` in the same
      run). Proximity, not duplication: the comment-policy-sweep entry above for
      `daemon/cmd/symmetry-fake-agent/autonomous.go` records the *unblockable* carrier, whose reader is
      never joined — a different property, not this test's failure.

      Update (2026-11-25, branch `refactor/rf-flake-autonomous-return-reader`): two further reds of the
      same test and assertion in the hosted `daemon` job's race step, each the only failing test of its
      run (`failed=1 package_fail=1`, and no `found unexpected goroutines:` line in either log) — run
      `45510612412` head `01c0d4cf` (`--- FAIL: TestAutonomousReturnReleasesItsInputReader (0.00s)`,
      `goroutine 47 [runnable]` in `runAutonomous.func1` at `autonomous.go:111 +0x213`) and run
      `45430210878` head `5c297c25` (`(0.04s)`, `goroutine 46 [runnable]`, same frame and offset). Cause
      named from the two dumps and a disassembly of a race-built test binary at the base (go1.27.4, the
      CI flags class): both runs returned well under `readerJoinTimeout = 50 ms`, so the join returned
      through `<-readerDone` (the reader had run its deferred `close(readerDone)`), and `+0x213` is the
      `CALL runtime.racefuncexit(SB)` immediately after the `CALL runtime.deferreturn(SB)` that runs the
      reader's `close(inputs)`/`close(readerDone)` defers — the assertion read a released reader inside
      its death transition; instantaneous presence is not a leak.

      **Closed (2026-11-25, branch `refactor/rf-flake-autonomous-return-reader`, plan
      `docs/plans/2026-11-25-rf-flake-autonomous-return-reader-plan.md`):** the in-test scan now dumps
      all stacks after the return and re-dumps every 1 ms until the reader frame vanishes or the named
      `readerExitSettle = 1 s` bound passes; only persistence past the bound reds, and the failure detail
      carries `runElapsed` (a sub-`readerJoinTimeout` return self-classifies as a released reader). The
      pins: a ~50 ms post-release reader tail reds the old check 5/5 (with `runElapsed` 5.07 ms) and
      passes the new check 50/50; a product dropping the input `Close()` reds the new check 3/3 on the
      settle deadline (parked `io.PipeReader.Read` frame in the dump, and goleak reports it too) with the
      old check also 3/3; a reader whose error branch never exits reds the new check 3/3 (spinning
      `runtime.Gosched` frame, and goleak too) with the old check also 3/3; removing the join's
      `time.After` arm still reds the sibling bound test at its 30 s guard. Loaded repeat
      5 red/20000 before → 0 red/20000 after, and the WSL `-race` oracle
      exits 0. Product files untouched (`autonomous.go` blob `d82b5991…`), no assertion removed, no
      bound raised, no skip. Evidence: `docs/evidence/rf-flake-autonomous-return-reader-2026-11-25.md`.
      CI `daemon`/`daemon-windows` on the PR head: pending (conductor-owned).

## rf-acc-clone-cleanup-parent-death: the installed-owner leg's parent-death stop (2026-11-25)

Moved 2026-11-25 from `docs/TODO.md`'s `## rf-acc-native-burndown: Linux v2 native acceptance
follow-ups (2026-11-24)` (PR #287); the two sibling stops stay open there under the same heading.

- [ ] Root-cause the installed-owner leg's parent-death stop (2026-11-24, goal
      `rf-acc-native-burndown`; run `45402706492`, evidence §3.15):
      `daemon/native/linux-gate/protocol/clone_cleanup_linux_test.go:259` (`A cleanup caller exited
      without the requested parent-death signal`; run `45398550682` stopped one check earlier at
      `:207`, cleared by the fixture's six-digit `z-later-` name fix, §3.14/§3.15).
      **Closed (2026-11-25, branch `refactor/rf-acc-clone-cleanup-parent-death`, base `6b2d4f8f`;
      plan `docs/plans/2026-11-25-rf-acc-clone-cleanup-parent-death-plan.md`, evidence record
      `docs/evidence/2026-11-25-rf-acc-clone-cleanup-parent-death.md`):** the stop was the
      predicate's channel — `os.Process.Wait`'s error is nil for any successfully reaped exit,
      signal deaths included, so `waitErr == nil` fired on the caller's requested SIGKILL — named in
      the field as closed values (stage-1 run `45597242823`: caller `signaled=true signal=9
      exit=-1 core=false wait_error=false`; root deleter `si_signo=17 si_code=2 si_status=9`) and
      fixed by reading the reaped status instead (`signaled && signal == SIGKILL`, original message
      kept; the root deleter asserted through the fence's siginfo instead of pidfd readiness). Run
      `45600764607` passed the test (`fail_events` 4 → 3, test absent from `bad=`), the conductor's
      Bind run `45611900578` bound the delivered `ac0fa101` bytes (same pass, next stops named
      verbatim), and no product byte, fixture, bound or assertion class moved (the privileged
      clone-cleanup set is byte-identical to the base). PR #287's round-1 findings R1-R5
      (live-probe cleanup, EINTR poll count, dead store, address re-derivation, this move) are
      applied on the branch; the re-bind on the round-1 head follows.

Moved 2026-11-26 from `docs/TODO.md`'s `## rf-acc-native-burndown: Linux v2 native acceptance
follow-ups (2026-11-24)`; the same goal's new residual entries stay open in `docs/TODO.md` under
`## rf-acc-containment-goal-admission: residuals (2026-11-26)`.

- [ ] Decide the Linux v2 native owner witness's clone-admission contradiction (2026-11-24, goal
      `rf-acc-native-containment-witness`; evidence
      `docs/evidence/rf-acc-native-containment-witness-2026-11-24.md` §7.2, runs `45452357027`,
      `45455325059`, `45458181149`): with the captured App stderr now visible, both containment
      sets stop after the claim seed with `reserve owner witness clone before source Git effects:
      Linux clone intent does not match the current admitted Subject`. The witness requires a
      `git_clone_v2` workspace (`daemon/internal/app/linux_owner_witness_linux.go:2981`) whose
      reservation demands an admitted Control Subject
      (`daemon/internal/state/linux_clone_journal.go:208-216,864-878`), while the witness request
      validation refuses a claim that carries an admission
      (`daemon/e2e/production_linux_v2_native_witness_test.go:1565-1567`: `owner-witness claim must
      be a real non-Goal Control task`) and Control's `tasks_goal_fields_all_or_none` forbids goal
      fields on a goal-less task (`scripts/ci/provision-linux-v2-control-claims.exs:415-421`); no
      fixture claim satisfies both, so the rows never reach the credential-dependent step. Smallest
      proposed fix: run the witness on a Goal-backed admitted claim whose admission Subject equals
      the request Subject — relax the harness check to `present && admission.Subject ==
      request.Subject` and provision the fixture's tasks through the Goal/admission flow (pattern
      `scripts/ci/omo-control-native-fixture.exs:190,262-284,297-303,376`) — which grows the owned
      set by that harness block. The alternative, exempting the witness from the shared clone-v2
      admission guard, would weaken a production guard (`daemon/internal/**`).
      **Closed (2026-11-26, goal `rf-acc-containment-goal-admission`, branch
      `refactor/rf-acc-containment-goal-admission`, base `4440c5c0`; plan
      `docs/plans/2026-11-25-rf-acc-containment-goal-admission-plan.md`, evidence record
      `docs/evidence/rf-acc-containment-goal-admission-2026-11-25.md`):** the four containment
      cases (`codex_containment`, `claude_containment`, `stage_replay_native_release_codex`,
      `stage_replay_native_release_claude`) now run on the same Control-composed Goal admission the
      pi-durability cases already use — the fixture routes them through `provision_goal_admission!`
      with the OpenCode repository resource, the writer keys its admission branch on the goal case
      set, the codex/claude claim runtimes carry native-harness adapter metadata, and the e2e
      predicate `linuxV2WitnessRequiresGoalAdmission` keys the admission branch on the goal-routed
      request cases (the pi-durability rows plus the codex/claude rows), leaving every other
      provider-free row on the non-Goal refusal; `daemon/internal/**` stayed byte-identical and no
      assertion was weakened outside the conductor ruling
      (`refactor/decision-witness-admission-2026-11-25.md`).
      Scratch runs `45665341553` and `45668197894` (earlier rounds, their own bytes) observe both
      containment cases (`codex_containment`, `claude_containment`) past the clone reserve with
      zero `reserve owner witness clone` / `clone intent does not match` refusals; the Bind run
